**Date:** 2026-06-14. **Status:** Design converged (conversation), not yet implemented. Full spec: [design/copyflow-layer.md](../design/copyflow-layer.md). The copy/diff/apply refinement of [public-layering.md](../design/plans/public-layering.md); a consumer of the substrate (D84); persistence per D85.

**Problem.** Design copyflow (today's `internal/copyflow`, a procedural free-function package) as a clean public refinement: its handle shape, the copy-vs-overlay tangle, multi-dir (`--all`) behavior, the host↔substrate boundary, and persistence — all consistent with the substrate model and security-first.

**Decision.** Copyflow is the **review workflow** (protect / review / land), a refinement of *transfer* that mediates between the **work copy** (at/in the substrate) and the **host original**. Resolutions: (1) a **per-tracked-dir handle** (the atom) with uniform verbs (`Diff`/`Apply`/`AdvanceBaseline`/`Export`/`History`), replacing the `(layout,rt,name,dirHostPath)` threading; (2) **seeding vs propagation** — the dir mode decides whether a divergeable work copy exists (`copy`/`overlay` yes; `rw`/`ro` no), and change propagation is uniform (produce diff+metadata, replay on the original), only *transport* varies (same-fs git vs cross-fs intermediaries) — dissolves the copy/overlay "tangle" without a strategy abstraction; (3) **repo-aware resolution** — repo dir → commit series + tags onto its own original repo; non-repo → bare changes (metadata = commits+tags, repo-only); (4) **`--all` is a collection of independent per-dir resolutions, NEVER a merge** (two repos = two DAGs); `apply --all` lands each separately, `diff --all` is a per-dir breakdown; the single combined absolute-path patch is demoted to a lossy opt-in export; (5) **characterize-and-surface** for nature mismatch — commits-beyond-baseline on a non-repo original → a typed signal, user decides (apply-flat / abort-and-make-repo); copyflow never silently flattens history nor auto-converts the original's nature; (6) **hermetic-git security seal** — the in-sandbox git is untrusted, **read+emit only, never writes outside the sandbox**; changes egress only as diff+metadata via a read-only channel (`Exec` stdout / `GetPath`); the **trusted host-side git** applies, path-confined; two layers of distrust (binary sealed, content confined); (7) **copyflow owns its baseline persistence** (leaves `store.Environment` → a copyflow record via the shared helper — the D85 instance); (8) **substrate consumption** — `Exec` (produce-side git egress), `PutPath` (seed), host git (apply); `FilesystemLocality` injected into the produce-side executor so verbs don't branch; "needs box running" derivable from locality, auto-start is caller policy.

**Rejected.** Merging tracked dirs into one diff (incoherent across distinct repos; loses commits+tags); a `ChangeCapture` strategy interface for copy-vs-overlay (the seeding/propagation split is cleaner); copyflow auto-starting a stopped box or auto-converting a bare original into a repo (both caller/user policy).

**Consequences.** A per-dir `TrackedDir` handle (name TBD) + a characterized `Resolution` type (commits+tags vs bare, with a nature-mismatch signal); copyflow gains its own persisted record; the hermetic seal becomes a stated security invariant + a verify-the-current-code finding (DF35 — the `git.NewSandbox` apply paths). Existing machinery (`GenerateFormatPatch`/`ApplyFormatPatch`, tags, `ListCommitsBeyondBaseline`) carries over; the model correction is *never merge across dirs; each resolution stays whole and lands on its own original.* Next session: the config/persistence helper (foundation, generalizes D85).

## D87 — Persistence helper: scoped versioned handles over per-domain single docs; flock + atomic-rename; daemon-optional

**Date:** 2026-06-15. **Status:** Design converged (conversation), not yet implemented. Full spec: [design/persistence-helper.md](../design/persistence-helper.md). Foundation persistence for [public-layering.md](../design/plans/public-layering.md) (library *and* tools); generalizes D85; backed by [research/shared-state-concurrency.md](../design/research/shared-state-concurrency.md). Extensively stress-tested across the design conversation.

**Problem.** Each layer/component must persist its own facts (D85/D86), repeated everywhere — needs a shared mechanism. And `~/.yoloai` is bifurcated into library vs tool ownership (D60) to let multiple tools (CLI + MCP) access the same sandboxes — so persistence must support multi-tool concurrent access safely. Open questions: file structure, versioning/migration across multi-version spans, concurrency, and whether file-locks suffice vs a daemon.

**Decision.** A **scoped, versioned persistence *handle*** is the core abstraction: a component reads/writes only its own slice through a handle, blind to the rest *including its physical location* — so the handle is the membrane that makes the representation swappable and stops migrations rippling. Resolutions: (1) **config follows the instance tree** (scoped by position, not type — yoloAI's per-dir copyflow = N copyflow slices); `Sub(name)` derives child scopes; (2) **single doc per ownership *domain*** — components that version together share a doc (the library = one doc/sandbox), domains that version independently are separate files (library / cli / mcp — the D60 bifurcation); granularity = one doc per (domain × sandbox); (3) **migration = one monotonic plain-int version + an append-only, ordered registry of raw-JSON steps**, replayed `current+1..latest` in historical order (independent per-tier counters can't sequence cross-tier redistributions, so they collapse to one version line); authorship local, sequencing global; redistribution steps (root-owned, cross-section) run before content steps; (4) **no auto-migration — balk and fail fast**; explicit `yoloai system migrate` only (D61 philosophy; prevents the silent ratchet); steps immutable-once-shipped; (5) **the version field is sacred** (fixed top-level plain int, never relocated, sole compatibility arbiter — so *every* schema change bumps it, even additive, because `json.Unmarshal` silently drops unknown fields → a lagging binary would lose them); (6) **doc-only migration is transactional for free** (replay in memory, one atomic write; crash → pristine prior version); fs restructuring is out-of-band; (7) **concurrency: `flock` (not `fcntl`) + atomic-rename** → lock-free `Load`, mutate only via `Update` (lock → re-read fresh → version+CAS re-check → write); (8) **library/tool boundary = single-source-of-truth boundary** — library owns what all tools must agree on (substrate config, copyflow baselines, agent config/status, the prompt); tools own private/recoverable views; tests: "must other tools agree?" and "needs atomic coupling with the sandbox's reality? → it's library"; tool state must be independently recoverable, never cross-domain atomically co-updated; (9) **notification is reactivity not correctness** (locks give correctness; agent status already polls by container-boundary necessity; proactive reactivity = poll or `inotify`-on-the-rename, daemon-free); (10) **daemon stays optional/deferred** — coordination lives below the library API, so file-locks-now / on-demand-service-later is swappable; daemonless by default (no SPOF/root daemon).

**Rejected.** A daemon as the baseline (file-locks suffice inside our envelope — single host, low contention, whole-doc ops; research-backed); SQLite now (adds a CGO/dep against "just the binary", doesn't escape the NFS limit, and is for the multi-file/high-concurrency cases we structurally avoid — but swappable behind the handle later); independent per-component/layout version counters (can't sequence multi-version cross-tier replay); auto-migration on load (the silent ratchet); per-component files (multi-file migration loses single-write atomicity, and the lock is already per-sandbox so finer files buy nothing).

**Consequences.** Largely a reframe of `store` (its `flock` is already the shared lock): the generic atomic-sudo-safe IO + version guard + domain/filename registry become the shared mechanism; substrate's `Environment`/`SandboxState` become *its* records. New findings: **DF36** (warn/refuse on network FS), **DF37** (verify `flock`-not-`fcntl` + add the fsync-durability dance). Survived stress tests for atomicity, durability, stale locks, lost updates, version mismatch (both directions), partial + multi-version migration, concurrency, daemon-vs-locks, NFS, lock-exhaustion, notification, and cross-tool version coupling — each reinforcing the same shape. Home/name of the package deferred (low-stakes behind the `Handle` interface).

## D88 — The session layer: a durable I/O channel (`IOSession`) over an agent process; the agent session carves off the substrate's neutral PID 1

**Date:** 2026-06-23. **Status:** Design converged (conversation), not yet implemented. Full spec: [design/session-layer.md](../design/session-layer.md). The **session** refinement of [public-layering.md](../design/plans/public-layering.md) and the module-split's deferred "C-full"; a consumer of the substrate (D84). Driven by the **control-eval** AI-control eval harness as the first real headless consumer. Backed by [research/container-init-delineation.md](../design/research/container-init-delineation.md).

**Problem.** tmux is the codebase's biggest tangle (~70 files) doing five conflated jobs (PTY provision, persistence, reattach, inject, capture), and the agent session *is* the container's PID 1 — so the substrate underneath can't be agent-free (DF31), and its config DTO mixes substrate + agent fields (DF33). C-minimal lifted `TmuxSocket`/`AttachCommand` onto an `InteractiveSession` interface but left it tmux-shaped. Open: the real session abstraction; how much moves Go-side; the completion signal (control-eval's P1 — the idle heuristics trip on a startup blip); re-launch semantics; security of driving/observing an untrusted agent.

**Decision.** A durable, reattachable **`IOSession`** — four ops (attach·inject·capture·persist) over `SessionKind {PTY, Stream}` — is the abstraction; it is a *channel*, not a process (the `-p` one-shot runs with none). Resolutions: (1) **three axes, kept orthogonal** — `SessionKind` (PTY broker / Stream stdio-files; Stream deferred, real-demand), `PromptMode` (how the prompt arrives), and new **`lifetime {one-shot, persistent}`** (caller intent; one-shot has two realizations by agent capability — `claude -p`→substrate `Launch`+`Wait(exit)`, or composition over a persistent `IOSession` for TUI-only agents); (2) **the carve** — a neutral PID 1 keep-alive (`tini`/`pause`, promoting seatbelt/tart's existing P1 split to the default); the agent session demotes from "the init" to a Go-driven `Substrate.Launch` (neutral PID 1 ≠ no in-container helpers — they become session-scoped launched processes); closes DF31; (3) **coarse `Launch`** — one Launch per session (PTY→a slimmed in-container session process bringing up broker+agent+monitor; Stream→the bare agent), Go owns *policy* not in-container mechanism; inject/capture/attach are host-side Go wrapping `Substrate.Exec`; (4) **the Launch unit differs by `SessionKind`** — PTY tracks the broker (idle/done = monitor sidecar), Stream tracks the agent (so **exit=done is Stream-only**), keeping substrate liveness and agent-status cleanly separate; (5) **three-bucket schema** — `ProvisionSpec` (substrate, agent-free) / neutral `ProcSpec` (Launch input) / `AgentLaunchSpec` (agent layer, compiled into a `ProcSpec` + a staged agent-config artifact); agent fields never enter a substrate type (closes DF33; refines D84's "agent layer's `ProcSpec`" into two types); (6) **monitor relocated not rewritten** — the in-container detector stack stays, started as a sibling, writing the D85 status sidecar; no monitor on the Stream/`-p` path; (7) **three completion tiers** — idle-heuristic (shipped) → **tier-2 authoritative idle** (the agent's turn-stop hook; per-agent capability; **hook-authoritative-exclusive** kills the startup blip; deferred `hook-unreliable` escape-hatch mode via a per-agent mode-selector seam built now; **turn-cursor contract** — `Wait(idle)` waits for completed-turn > submit-turn, blip-proof) → exit=done (tier-3); (8) **re-launch** — restart/stop-start/create are three ops by which-layer-cycles; restart re-runs the launch with the substrate *up*; `lifetime` decides prompt replay (one-shot re-injects, persistent doesn't); **persistent restart = fresh agent** (environment persists + cross-agent parity: fresh is the LCD, native resume is a deferred per-agent capability); the trust prompt is a fresh-*environment* event, so DF13 dissolves; (9) **naming `IOSession`** ("IO" disambiguates from the agent's conversation "session" and signals channel-agnostic); (10) **security = a one-way trust valve** (mirror of D86's seal, protecting the host terminal+artifacts): capture is tainted-at-the-boundary — live attach is pass-through (ssh-to-untrusted-host model), snapshot/bug-report egress is sanitize-escapes + best-effort-redact + opt-in; inject is argv-parameterized opaque (never host-shell-interpreted); inject *authorization* is the embedder's (D58/D59); all accepted gated on **load-bearing risk documentation**.

**Rejected.** A host-side Go monitor driving capture via `Exec` (rewrites proven logic; a round-trip per poll); fine-grained Go-driven in-container choreography (chatty/fragile); `ProcSpec` carrying ready/idle (re-pollutes the substrate type — DF33 reopens at the Launch boundary); native conversation-resume as the re-launch *baseline* (Claude-shaped; re-couples the abstraction); hook-merged-with-heuristics as the *default* (the heuristics are what trip the startup blip); a sanitizing live-attach mode (you cannot sanitize a live TUI without breaking it); `Session`/`TerminalSession`/`Channel` as the handle name (conversation-collision / channel-shaped / Go-`chan` clash). Stream `SessionKind` and the `hook-unreliable` mode are deferred (real-demand / build-if-testing-shows-flaky), with seams so they slot in without rework.

**Consequences.** The agent session lifts off PID 1; ~70 tmux touchpoints collapse to one handle; the substrate becomes genuinely agent-free (DF31/DF33 close). The shipped tier-1 `wait`/`run` surface gains reliability transparently when an agent is hook-capable. Spec-time to-dos: confirm the current `Stop`-hook wiring; name the internal in-container session process (`session-runner`/`session-setup`); audit the inject escaping; enumerate the required doc surfaces. Next design refinements in the cluster: netpolicy, envsetup, the agent layer — then Shape (code behind `internal/`) and Move (promote).

## D89 — The agent layer: per-agent adapters (mechanism vs payload) over the agent-free lower layers; file-defined agents open

**Date:** 2026-06-24. **Status:** Design converged (conversation), not yet implemented. Full spec: [design/agent-layer.md](../design/agent-layer.md). The **agent** refinement of [public-layering.md](../design/plans/public-layering.md); the agent-aware half of DF32. Backed by [research/agent-global-context.md](../design/research/agent-global-context.md).

**Problem.** The agent "layer" does not exist as a layer: a flat 24-field `Definition` (`internal/agent`) plus agent-aware logic scattered across `invocation` (model/command/detector), `provision` (seed files/secrets/settings), `create`+`context`, `network`, `discovery`, and `state`. After the carve made substrate/session/copyflow agent-free (D84/D88/D86), this scatter is what's left to gather. Open: the layer's shape; where each agent-specific bit belongs; whether agents are an open or closed set; the public surface; and (a verified-facts question) how each agent ingests global context, so the design isn't surprised by an incompatible shape.

**Decision.** The agent layer is a set of **per-agent adapters**, governed by one principle: **an agent declares the MECHANISM (how it does a thing); the consuming layer supplies the PAYLOAD (what to do); the agent owns its mechanisms + its own static self-config, never another layer's payload.** Resolutions: (1) the flat `Definition` clusters into **capability groups, each owned by exactly one consuming layer** — completion, launch, model, credentials, network, context, self-config; (2) a declaration is **data + a thin typed code-adapter** for the residual procedural bits (settings array-merge, opencode validation), fully-data deferred; (3) **re-homing is a three-way sort** — the agent keeps its declaration, the cross-layer payload leaves for its owner, and the generic *runner* (detector-loop, seed-stager, policy-composer, prompt-deliverer) lives in the *consuming* layer parameterized by the declaration — so the agent layer is *thinner* than "gather all agent logic" (the runners distribute outward; closes the agent-aware half of DF32). Boundaries pinned with unbuilt layers: completion ← the status-writer command + turn-cursor; netpolicy ← the domain-floor; envsetup ← seed-staging + settings-write (credentials shed entirely — shape→agent, values→caller, staging→envsetup); (4) **Context = a purely-internal `DEF`-injection method** — the user's global config is already seeded by Credentials, so Context never reaches outside; the agent declares its injection method (append-at-`StateDir`/`ContextFile` for 4 agents, a `--read` launch-flag for Aider — both data; survey-verified), and yoloAI's collected operating context `DEF` is a fan-in of each concerned layer's fragment; (5) **openness — the file/data path is open now** (`~/.yoloai/agents/<name>.yaml`, own files, *trusted config*): a file carries only the data-expressible subset, so the **file format is the data/code boundary by construction**; the code-adapter (Claude settings-merge, opencode validation) stays internal, deferred to a real code-needing third-party agent; (6) **public surface = type vs instance** — the type is a read-only capability *catalog* (`yoloai.AgentTypes()` → `AgentInfo` enriched with the capability flags an embedder needs); the instance `sb.Agent()` is a *composition* join (identity + prompt + status + `.IOSession()`, D88) in the root package; **separability** (use sandboxing without agent machinery) is the reason the layer is opt-in, guaranteed by the dependency direction `agent → substrate/session/copyflow`, never back. The **package boundary** is a corollary of re-homing + separability; the code-move is Shape-phase.

**Rejected.** A closed registry kept indefinitely ("closed now, seam-shaped" — revised once the capability model made the data path cheap; we open the file path now rather than dancing around it); fully-data declarations now (the append-aware patch format + declarative validation — deferred); a standalone public `agent` *registration* package before openness (the package exists for *separability* regardless; only its registration API waited); folding file-defined agents into the existing profile config (an agent is a different noun — a profile *selects* an agent — so own files); treating the global-context shape as uniform (the survey caught Aider's structural divergence — no auto-read global file — before it could surprise the design).

**Consequences.** The agent layer ends up small — per-agent *data + tiny adapters*; the runners and payloads live in the consuming layers (completion/netpolicy/envsetup/session). Findings surfaced for Shape: the Q&A-protocol injection must become agent-agnostic (today Claude-hardcoded, `context.go:177`), append-don't-clobber the seeded config, the injected block is the weakest context layer (not authoritative). New research note `research/agent-global-context.md` (verified mid-2026): 4 agents fit "append to one home-dir markdown file", Aider needs a `--read` declaration; per-agent footguns (Gemini configurable name, Codex `AGENTS.override.md`+`CODEX_HOME`, OpenCode `CLAUDE.md` fallback); `@`-imports are Claude/Gemini-only; `AGENTS.md` is the convergent standard. File-defined-agents work = schema + loader + validator. Next design refinements in the cluster: netpolicy, envsetup — then Shape and Move.

## D90 — Netpolicy: the network-policy refinement; a pluggable enforcement-strategy axis with an out-of-sandbox point reserved for hostile containment

**Date:** 2026-06-24. **Status:** Design converged (conversation), not yet implemented. Full spec: [design/netpolicy.md](../design/netpolicy.md). The **netpolicy** refinement of [public-layering.md](../design/plans/public-layering.md); resolves DF34; consumes the agent layer's domain-floor (D89).

**Problem.** Network isolation (CNI, netns, iptables, ipset) is woven into the backends (DF34) and should be a standalone refinement over the substrate. And — surfaced by mapping the implementation — the current allowlist is an **IP-approximation** (resolve domains→IPs once at startup, ipset + iptables default-deny, in-sandbox) that leaks for an adversarial agent; the security-research direction needs *hostile* network containment, which the structure must accommodate.

**Decision.** Netpolicy **composes** an egress policy and **enforces** it over the substrate via a **pluggable strategy**. Resolutions: (1) **the carve splits provisioning from policy** — the in-container iptables enforcement is already backend-agnostic, so *network provisioning* (containerd's CNI netns/bridge/IP) is a **substrate** concern, and *policy + enforcement* is netpolicy; (2) **policy is domain-centric and agnostic** — `mode {none,isolated,full}` is intent, `isolated` ⇒ `agent-floor ∪ user-allow \ user-deny` with provenance; domain-centric is forward-compatible because L7 is natively domain-based and the IP-approximation is the *degradation*; (3) **enforcement is a strategy axis** — `ip-filter` (in-sandbox iptables, now) and `egress-proxy` (the committed future); **"hostile" = a new strategy, not a new mode**; (4) **the load-bearing structural room: the enforcement *point* is NOT assumed in-sandbox** — the contract must allow a host-side / out-of-agent-control point as first-class, because that assumption is the one thing that, if baked in, forces a rewrite when the proxy lands (hostile containment is *enforcement outside the agent's reach by construction*, not a better filter); (5) **capability is per-(backend × strategy)** — `ip-filter` = containers/Linux-VM only; `egress-proxy` is host-side and *potentially* covers seatbelt/tart's current no-op; **characterize-and-surface** unenforceable policy rather than silently no-op; (6) **honest characterization** — `ip-filter` is a best-effort domain allowlist (DNS-exfil, shared-IP, stale-IP, IPv4-only, in-sandbox-tamper), explicitly **not** adversarial containment; `none` is the only hard boundary today.

**Rejected.** Folding network logic into the substrate/backend (the DF34 status quo); a "hostile" *mode* alongside none/isolated/full (it is an enforcement *strategy*, orthogonal to intent); assuming in-sandbox enforcement (the assumption that would block the proxy); building the `egress-proxy` now (committed but slotted as a feature *after* the revamp — the structure leaves room, the build is deferred); over-promising `isolated` as adversarial containment.

**Consequences.** The current `entrypoint.py:isolate_network` becomes netpolicy's `ip-filter` strategy, applied at provision/launch (off the D88-carved entrypoint); containerd's CNI becomes substrate network-provisioning. The `egress-proxy` strategy slots in later with no policy-model or contract change. seatbelt/tart `isolated` must be surfaced as unenforceable (today a silent no-op) and may be *fixed* by the future proxy. Next design refinements in the cluster: **envsetup** — then Shape and Move.

## D91 — Envsetup: the inside-the-sandbox environment preparer (dual of the substrate); the security home for credential crossing

**Date:** 2026-06-24. **Status:** Design converged (conversation), not yet implemented. Full spec: [design/envsetup.md](../design/envsetup.md). The **envsetup** refinement of [public-layering.md](../design/plans/public-layering.md); the runner for the agent layer's credential/self-config/context capabilities (D89). The last design-cluster refinement — the design phase is now complete.

**Problem.** The agent-environment staging (credentials/secrets, the agent's home-config seed, settings patches, the `DEF` context injection, the resolved env) is scattered across `provision/` and agent-aware. The re-homing (D89) routed it all "→ envsetup" without yet shaping the layer. And credentials physically *enter* the sandbox in this staging — so the deferred credential-delivery findings (DF38 secure delivery, DF39 the `$HOME` bleed) need a home and a seam, acutely so for the metered-JV-key + adversarial-agent direction.

**Decision.** Envsetup is the **dual of the substrate**: substrate provisions the agent-free shell, envsetup provisions its agent-specific *contents*, **host-side**, before the neutral container runs (the D88 carve). Resolutions: (1) it stages secrets, seed files (where the user global config `ABC` lands), settings patches, the assembled context `DEF`, and the agent runtime env — from `{agent shapes + caller values + DEF}`; (2) it is **mostly a gathering** of today's `provision/` logic, since the contract was pre-pinned by the re-homing (D89), the credential what/who (D63: caller `Env`, zero ambient reads, `SecretsStagingDir`), and the carve (D88); (3) **separability via an agnostic `EnvSpec`** — envsetup consumes an agent-*produced* spec (credential-shape + seed-list + settings-patches + context-method + resolved env), not the agent package, just as substrate takes `ProvisionSpec` and session `ProcSpec`; (4) **envsetup is the security *home* for credential crossing** — both the secrets staging (caller values → bind-mounted dir, DF38) and the seed-file copy (the agent's host config, which may carry credentials, DF39) cross here; (5) **baseline now + secure-secrets seam** (à la netpolicy's proxy) — establish the D63 baseline and leave structural room so the build can be deferred: the staging mechanism must **not assume** "plaintext files into a bind-mounted dir" is the only path (a broker / ephemeral injection / no-host-creds path must slot in), and the host-config seed must be able to become **opt-in / filtered** rather than wholesale-copy.

**Rejected.** Leaving the staging scattered/agent-welded in `provision/`; envsetup depending on the agent package directly (it consumes the agnostic `EnvSpec` instead); baking the plaintext-files-bind-mount as the only credential path (the assumption that would force a rewrite when the secure-secrets model lands); building the secure-secrets delivery / opt-in-host-config now (committed but deferred — the seam is shaped, the build waits, like netpolicy's egress-proxy).

**Consequences.** The scattered `provision/` functions (`CreateSecretsDir`, `CopySeedFiles`, `EnsureContainerSettings`, the `DEF` append, env resolution) gather into envsetup, parameterized by the agent-produced `EnvSpec`. DF38/DF39 now have a home and a seam. **The design cluster is complete** (substrate D84/D85, copyflow D86, persistence D87, session D88, agent D89, netpolicy D90, envsetup D91) — the program moves to **Shape** (write the code behind `internal/`) and **Move** (the mechanical `git mv` promotion).

## D92 — Pre-Shape design-review remediation: code-verified the cluster, killed false alarms, re-homed the entrypoint orphan, six-backend honesty

**Date:** 2026-06-24. **Status:** Applied (spec edits + new findings). A cross-cutting review of the converged public-layering cluster (D84–D91) before the Shape phase — three adversarial design reviewers (seam integrity / cross-layer contracts / holes & coverage) plus four code-verification agents, then this remediation. New reference: [design/backend-topology.md](../design/backend-topology.md).

**Problem.** Seven layers converged across many sessions; before writing code, verify the seams hold, the cross-layer contracts are consistent, and there are no holes — and **research every load-bearing assumption against the code** rather than inherit reviewer claims.

**Decision (the verified outcome).** **No would-force-rewrite seam and no broken core abstraction** — the liveness/status split, mechanism/payload principle, capability model, and persistence handle all hold. The reviews surfaced real gaps; code verification then **killed several false alarms** and **shrank the rest**:
- **Killed:** "`none` is a no-op on macOS" — FALSE; `none` enforces natively (seatbelt SBPL deny-default, tart `--net-softnet-block`). **DF35 hermetic-git seal** — VERIFIED CLEAN (every `git.NewSandbox` is read/emit-only; writers are `git.NewHost`-only). "CNI netns teardown unowned" — FALSE (`teardownCNI` runs on stop+destroy). "seatbelt/tart host-process leak" — FALSE (reaped via `killByPID`/pgrep; DF40 is *terminal corruption*, not a leak). "fail-open risk" — FALSE (already fail-closed).
- **Confirmed + applied as spec edits:** (1) **the entrypoint orphan** — the carve would orphan four agent-free `entrypoint.py` root ops; re-homed: UID-remap + the in-container overlay-mount → **substrate**, `isolate_network` → **netpolicy** (`ip-filter`), secrets-read + `.secrets-consumed` handshake → **envsetup** ([DF41](../design/findings-unresolved.md)/[DF42](../design/findings-unresolved.md)); (2) **six-backend topology** — all six run the *same* `sandbox-setup.py` so the carve is uniform; corrected the "seatbelt/tart run on the host" error (only seatbelt is a host process; tart/containerd/apple are VM guests; containerd is Kata; apple is OCI-in-VM); seatbelt is the lone `HostKeepAlive` case where §6's liveness split needs a host-process variant; (3) **netpolicy enforceability is per-(backend × isolation-mode × platform × strategy)** — folded the existing gVisor (`container-enhanced`) hard-reject + podman-macOS per-IP fallback into the capability model; the mutation *transport* (not just the enforcement *point*) is per-strategy; the proxy *mechanism* must be routing-enforced not env-cooperative (Q37); (4) **`DEF` has an assembler: envsetup**; (5) the in-container status sidecar (`agent-status.json`) is **outside** the persistence Handle/`flock`+`Update` contract (single in-container writer, host-polled), disambiguated from `agent.json`; (6) failure/atomicity/teardown + pipeline-ordering + the 3-way env split pinned as contracts; (7) credential-at-rest characterize-and-surface now ([DF43](../design/findings-unresolved.md)).

**Rejected.** Acting on reviewer claims without code verification (would have "fixed" working `none`-on-macOS and a sound hermetic seal); treating the carve as Linux-container-only (it's uniform — same `sandbox-setup.py` everywhere, only locality differs, which `KeepAliveModel`/`FilesystemLocality` already abstract).

**Consequences.** Specs edited: substrate (claims UID-remap + overlay-mount), session (backend-topology + seatbelt liveness variant + launch-readiness-timeout), netpolicy (four-axis matrix + verified `none` + mutation-transport + proxy-mechanism + persisted-home), envsetup (DEF assembler + secrets-handshake claim + atomicity + env-split + DF43 surface), persistence (status-sidecar exclusion), agent (hook-object shape + DEF→envsetup), copyflow (DF35 clean + the `--unsafe-paths`/raw-patch residual invariant). New findings DF41/DF42/DF43; DF35 resolved-verified. The cluster is now Shape-ready. **Next: Shape** (code behind `internal/`) then **Move** (promotion).

## D93 — E3: secrets delivered as launch env (Docker/Launch path); host `/tmp` staging + `.secrets-consumed` marker dissolved for Docker

**Date:** 2026-06-25. **Status:** Implemented + verified on real Docker (commit 163533a9). **Closes** the Docker half of [DF41](../design/findings-unresolved.md). **Downgrade trigger** for [DF43](../design/findings-unresolved.md). **Composition:** realizes the envsetup seam (D91) for the Docker backend; builds on the D88 carve (neutral PID 1 + `ProcessLauncher.Launch`) and D63 (caller-`Env` snapshot, zero ambient credential reads).

**The decision.** For the Docker/`ProcessLauncher` path, credentials are delivered as the **environment of the launched `sandbox-setup.py` process** (`ProcSpec.Env`), with a `YOLOAI_SECRET_KEYS` sentinel naming which vars are secrets — replacing the prior host-staged bind-mount at `/run/secrets`. This **eliminates the host-side at-rest surface entirely** for Docker: no `os.MkdirTemp` secrets dir, no `defer os.RemoveAll`, no `waitForSecretsConsumed` poll on the host, no `/run/secrets` bind-mount. The `.secrets-consumed` marker handshake — which existed solely to let the host know it was safe to delete the staged dir — **dissolves** because there is no host dir to tear down.

**Legacy backends** (containerd, tart, seatbelt — no `ProcessLauncher`) are **unchanged**: they still stage host files (`/run/secrets` bind-mount for containerd; persistent `<sandbox>/secrets/` for tart/seatbelt) and still write the `.secrets-consumed` marker, until they are carved.

**Threat-model framing (why this is the right decomposition).** Concealing an in-use credential from the agent is a non-goal (usable implies reachable). The wins here are: (a) **at-rest elimination** — the staged secret exists only in the container's env, not on the host filesystem; (b) **simplicity** — the handshake is a workaround for a problem (host-staged files needing cleanup) that no longer exists on the Docker path. The three durable secure-secrets goals (blast-radius/scope reduction, bystander-exclusion, exfil-cost reduction via broker/forwarding) are still valid and still deferred — [DF38](../design/findings-unresolved.md) (the secure broker/forwarding/scoping model) remains parked for dedicated security research.

**Why now / what the carve enabled.** The prior path had no `ProcessLauncher`: `sandbox-setup.py` was a baked-in entry-point that ran as PID 1, with no Go-level channel to inject an env at launch time. The D88 carve gave Docker a neutral keep-alive PID 1 and a Go-driven `Launch` that can pass `ProcSpec.Env` — exactly the delivery mechanism this decision uses. The at-rest surface didn't exist before to be eliminated; the carve is what created the opportunity.

**Two E3 loose ends** tracked as [DF47](../design/findings-unresolved.md): `sandbox-setup.py` still writes the now-vestigial `.secrets-consumed` marker on the Launch path (harmless litter); the `YOLOAI_SECRET_KEYS` sentinel is visible in the tmux env after startup (could be unset post-consumption).

**Rejected.** (a) Continuing the bind-mount approach on the Docker path — removes an elimination opportunity and keeps the host-side teardown logic that the carve made unnecessary. (b) Emitting a runtime warning for plaintext-at-rest — see DF43 disposition (D93 context): at-rest is a non-concern on the single-user/ephemeral model; the Docker path now has no host-staged file at all; the multi-principal concern is the embedder's via `SecretsStagingDir`, surfaced in integrator docs. (c) Changing legacy backends now — they lack a `ProcessLauncher` and the structural room for env-delivery; carve first.

## D94 — On-disk localization invariant: the binary writes nothing to the global temp dir; every path through `Layout`

**Date:** 2026-06-25. **Status:** Implemented (commit 36e7235f). **Composition:** applies D63's no-ambient-config discipline (zero ambient reads in the library) to the *write* direction: no ambient-global paths in the library either. Applies D58's "the library never synthesizes principal scope" to the temp-dir axis. Enables the multi-principal `SecretsStagingDir` knob (D63).

**The decision.** The yoloAI binary must write nothing to the global temp dir (`os.TempDir()` / `/tmp`) directly. Its entire footprint lives under one relocatable data dir threaded through `Layout`. Every `os.MkdirTemp("")` / `os.CreateTemp("")` in the library that formerly targeted `os.TempDir()` is rerouted through **`Layout.TempDir()`** (a `TempDir()` method on `Layout` that derives `<DataDir>/tmp/` or a caller-supplied override). Two installs with distinct `DataDir`s can coexist on the same host with no file-collision in `/tmp` — a prerequisite for the D62 multi-principal daemon (different principals' `DataDir`s are distinct).

**Why.** The original `os.MkdirTemp("")` calls were an ambient-global write: they created files under the OS temp dir, which is shared across all users and processes on the host. For the single-user CLI this is a minor hygiene issue (files are ephemeral, owned 0600). For a multi-principal daemon it is a correctness and isolation hazard: a principal's secrets staging dir ends up co-located in `/tmp` with another principal's, defeating the `SecretsStagingDir` isolation the D63 design promised. Threading every path through `Layout` is the write-side complement to the no-ambient-read invariant: the library's entire data footprint is auditable, relocatable, and principal-scoped.

**Rejected.** (a) A global `os.Setenv("TMPDIR", layout.TempDir())` shortcut — technically works, but it would be a foreign ambient-global mutation in a codebase that threads every path through `Layout`. The no-ambient discipline (D63, D79) bars writes to ambient globals as firmly as reads from them; setting `TMPDIR` is just a write to the same ambient-config channel we've been erasing. It would also affect child processes' temp dir without their knowledge. (b) Leaving the original `os.MkdirTemp("")` calls in place and relying on `SecretsStagingDir` as a per-call override — a partial fix that still leaks unrelated temp files to `/tmp` and makes the relocation guarantee conditional on callers remembering to set the override.

**Consequences.** `Layout.TempDir()` is the single site in the library that derives the temp dir; a `Layout` constructed with an explicit `TmpDir` override (the `SecretsStagingDir` knob is one such override) routes everything through it. The CLI's `DataDir` default (`~/.yoloai`) gets a `tmp/` subdir; two CLI installs under distinct `--data-dir` paths are fully isolated in their temp footprints. No public API change (the `SecretsStagingDir` `Options` field is unchanged; `Layout.TempDir()` is internal).

## D95 — Secure-secrets: the credential boundary is a host-side egress proxy that holds, injects, and refreshes credentials (DF38 design)

**Date:** 2026-06-25. **Status:** Designed (`docs/contributors/design/secure-secrets.md`); build phased. **Composition:** the credential half of D90's reserved `egress-proxy` strategy (one enforcement point for creds + network); extends D63's caller-`Env` credential contract; realizes D91 §5's secure-secrets seam; resolves DF38 + DF39; subsumes DF43 at-rest.

**The decision.** The durable credential boundary is a **host-side, out-of-agent-reach egress proxy** that holds the real credentials, injects them as headers on allowlisted egress (TLS-MITM with a yoloai CA the agent trusts via per-agent knobs — Claude `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, Codex `CODEX_CA_CERTIFICATE`), and **refreshes** short-lived tokens transparently — so the live credential **never enters the sandbox** and the agent never holds a token it can exfiltrate. It is the credential half of netpolicy's already-reserved `egress-proxy` strategy: one boundary-enforcement point for credentials AND network, on a different principal/namespace than the agent (the hostile-containment primitive — default-deny egress netns whose only outbound path is the proxy, enforced from outside the agent's sudo reach). Extends D63 with a refresh-capable **`CredentialSource`** (a static value OR a `Fetch → (value, expiresAt)` provider the embedder supplies): the embedder mints/scopes/revokes; yoloai delivers, injects, and orchestrates refresh.

**Why.** The realistic threat is a *duped* agent (confused deputy) prompt-injected into exfiltrating a credential via its normal tools; concealment is impossible (usable ⇒ reachable), so the goals are bound-blast-radius + make-exfil-hard + make-it-detectable (see [research/secure-credentials.md](../design/research/secure-credentials.md): the broker is the one novel control no shipping coding agent ships). **Token refresh is load-bearing and forces the architecture:** env/file delivery cannot refresh — a process env is fixed at launch, so a 1h token (Anthropic WIF `sk-ant-oat01`, GitHub App token) breaks a multi-hour session with no clean re-injection path. Because the proxy holds the credential and the agent never sees it, the proxy re-mints underneath an ongoing session transparently. So short-lived-token + long-session **requires** the proxy — a third independent argument for the broker, alongside no-host-at-rest and exfil-resistance. Today's copy/stage-a-key-the-agent-reads is the weakest option (a leaked key is reusable indefinitely); E3 removed the host at-rest plaintext for Docker, the proxy removes the in-sandbox exposure too.

**Rejected / deferred.** (a) Concealing the in-use key from the agent — a non-goal (usable ⇒ reachable). (b) Relying on env/file delivery + short-lived tokens for long sessions — structurally impossible (no refresh). (c) `HTTPS_PROXY` as the boundary — bypassable (the agent has sudo/`NET_ADMIN`); the boundary is the default-deny-egress netns + forced proxy, the env var is only a hint. (d) SSH agent forwarding as the git default — weaker (forwards a full-reach key, no native repo-scoping); a per-session repo-scoped token broker dominates it. (e) Building the proxy now — the **contract seam** is reserved now (EnvSpec credential-shape carrying destination+header+CA-knob; the `CredentialSource` abstraction; netpolicy's egress-proxy strategy already reserves the out-of-sandbox point), the proxy is built later with netpolicy's `egress-proxy` strategy. TLS pinning per provider/agent is an open spike (a pinned client can't be MITM'd).

**Consequences.** DF38 + DF39 are resolved-in-design (DF39: the `$HOME` credential mount becomes caller-controlled/filtered). The honest residual — a duped agent can still abuse in-scope capabilities during the live session (allowlisted requests, in-scope repo pushes, exfil through an over-broad allowlisted endpoint) — is defended **orthogonally** and must compose: the copy/diff/apply review gate (a human reviews the diff before it lands), a narrow SNI allowlist, and detection (per-sandbox egress logging + canary tokens). Baseline rule: the simple default must not be the dangerous one — direct env delivery is fine for the trusted/personal CLI case, brokering is the posture for untrusted/metered/multi-principal/long-session use (default policy TBD per the spec). control-eval (metered-JV-key + adversarial agent) is the concrete build driver.

## D96 — Agent-owned detection: the launch wrapper authoritatively writes `done` and falls to a resume-able shell

**Date:** 2026-06-25. **Status:** Designed (`docs/contributors/design/agent-detection.md`, Phase 0 of `plans/agent-owned-detection.md`); build phased (P1–P4). **Composition:** pins the concrete contracts for D88's fall-to-shell + tier-2 and D89's Resume capability; mirrors the `EnvSpec`/`ProcSpec` boundary pattern (D91).

**The decision.** Detection becomes the **agent's own declaration** — a Go `DetectionSpec` compiled from `agent.Definition` at the orchestrator boundary (new `detectspec.BuildDetectionSpec`, parallel to `envspec.BuildEnvSpec`), gathering the mode selector (`hook-authoritative`/`heuristic-only`/reserved `hook-assisted`), the heuristic params, the hook event-map, the new `Definition.ResumeCmd`, and a `FallToShell` rollout gate into one compiled artifact the in-container monitor reads. The launch shape changes from `exec {agent_command}` to `exec agent-run.sh {agent_command}`: a **single generic staged wrapper** runs the agent as a child, then **authoritatively writes `done`** with the agent's real exit code (reusing `status-monitor.py`'s `write_status` via a thin CLI — schema stays single-sourced + fenced by `schema_version_test.go`, never hand-rolled in shell), prints a resume hint, and `exec`s an interactive login shell so the pane stays usable instead of dying. A staged **`yoloai-resume`** re-launches the agent through the same wrapper with `ResumeCmd`, re-establishing detection in one command (the durable monitor — DF46 — is still running and re-detects). The detection runner **honors** the wrapper-written `done` (latches it like `in_done`, does not clobber it with the idle shell); in hook-authoritative mode this already holds for free (no heuristics while the pane lives), so **Phase 1 ships Claude-only with no monitor change** — the heuristic honor-change is Phase 3.

**Why.** Fall-to-shell (the declined-auto-relaunch alternative, D88) breaks the *only* `done` producer: pane death. Drop the `exec` and the pane never dies, so `done` never fires; worse, the heuristic monitor reads the idle shell as `idle`, masquerading an exited agent as waiting. So `done` must move to the wrapper (it knows the real exit happened and the real `$?`) and the runner must honor it. Detection is the most fragile, historically-troubled subsystem (DF44/DF46, the tier-2 blip, the stale-idle window), so the rollout is **gated** (`FallToShell` on for hook-authoritative, off for heuristic until Phase 3, gate removed Phase 4) and **phase-gated on real-Docker checkpoints** — the core mechanism (wrapper-writes-done + fall-to-shell) is validated at the cheapest point (Phase 1) before the DetectionSpec/heuristic rework. `done` here is **loose** (agent-process exited), not tier-3 session-over — a persistent/PTY session whose agent `/quit`s is not session-over (the pane lives as a shell); no new status value is introduced.

**Rejected / deferred.** (a) Inline `run; write-done; exec shell` one-liner — too fragile to escape with `ResumeCmd` flags embedded; stage a generic script parameterized by env. (b) Hand-rolling the `done` JSON in shell — would drift from the fenced v1 schema; reuse `write_status`. (c) Folding the durable monitor into the wrapper — keep the external-sibling runner (D88 §8, relocated-not-rewritten); two clean owners (wrapper = exit lifecycle, monitor = active/idle + latch). (d) Auto-relaunch on exit — declined (D88: conflates "exited" with "should run", crash-loops, breaks one-shot `lifetime`); resume is user-initiated. (e) A host `yoloai resume <name>` verb — YAGNI now (in-sandbox `yoloai-resume` is one command from the shell the user is in; `restart` covers host-side relaunch). (f) `--session-id` pinning — verify at Phase 2 whether Claude `--continue` (most-recent conversation in the workdir) suffices for the single-session sandbox before adding it.

**Consequences.** `Definition` gains `ResumeCmd`; `runtime-config.json` gains `fall_to_shell` (from the spec); one new staged `agent-run.sh` wrapper + one `yoloai-resume` script; the heuristic monitor learns the wrapper-`done` latch (Phase 3). The five tier-2 invariants are re-verified every phase (no blip, no stale-idle, `done` only on real exit, DF46 restart-brings-it-back, `DetectStatus` safe-default). Agents without native resume declare `ResumeCmd="none"` → the hint offers a fresh start and says so (honest characterization). Forward-compatible with the carve: the same wrapper is the launch `ProcSpec` pre- and post-carve, staged by envsetup.

**Refinement (2026-06-25) — detection as a strategy + the python-spine seam (reserved, not built).** DD2's `Mode` enum is the narrow form of a more general shape: **detection is a per-agent strategy defaulting to the heuristic stack** (an extension of D89 mechanism-vs-payload). Reserved seam (not built): (a) an agent with a native turn-completion callback supplies its own strategy (today only Claude's `Stop` hook); (b) the *implementation* of a code-needing strategy is a per-agent **namespaced python module loaded by convention** ("the spine") — and because detection runs *in the sandbox* (not the host library), this is a droppable python file a **file-defined agent could carry**, an openness refinement of D89 (which keeps only *host-side* procedural code internal); trust holds because in-sandbox detection is not a security boundary (`agent-status.json` is a host-read hint, the agent already runs code as the same user); (c) `hook-assisted` (hook + heuristic backstop) is the **one** reserved composite — general N-way stacking + winner-policy is YAGNI until a second composite appears. The load-bearing question — do Codex/Gemini/OpenCode/Aider expose a Claude-`Stop`-equivalent callback — is now **answered (verified 2026-06-25, `research/agent-callbacks.md`): all four do** (Codex `notify`/`agent-turn-complete`, Gemini `AfterAgent` GA ≥v0.26.0, OpenCode `session.idle` plugin/SSE, Aider's data-free `--notifications-command`). So the heuristic stack is the **floor** (legacy/unknown/file-defined), not the common case — the strategy seam is **worth building**. Two contract constraints fell out: callbacks come in **two families** (agent-runs-our-command vs we-subscribe-to-a-stream — cover both), and Aider's payload-free pulse means the contract is a **thin "a turn ended" signal**, not "hand me the message". Phase 3's `DetectionSpec` is shaped strategy-first so the enum doesn't have to be widened later. See [agent-detection.md](../design/agent-detection.md) DD2.

## D97 — Pre-Move audit: the substrate/store promotion needs a surface-cleanup Shape sub-phase first — it is not yet a pure `git mv`

**Date:** 2026-06-26. **Status:** Decided (audit of `plans/move-audit.md`, run against `git log main..public-layering` + the D84–D96 cluster + the code). Re-sequences Stage 4 ("Move") of [plans/public-layering.md](../design/plans/public-layering.md). **Method:** six audit dimensions fanned to read-only investigators; synthesized here.

**The finding.** The Move (S6) was framed as a *pure path change* — `git mv internal/runtime → yoloai/runtime` + `internal/store → yoloai/store` + import sweep — promotable "only when the contract has stabilized" (Frame strategy #2/#5). The audit's verdict: **the functional carve is done but the public *surface* is not yet agent-free, so a pure `git mv` today would freeze agent concepts into public semver.** The branch's S0–S3 carve (D88) IS built and verified — the Launch primitive (`runtime.ProcessLauncher`/`Process`, `docker/launch.go`), agent-free keepalive bring-up (`entrypoint.py` `keepalive_only`), and the agent-reroute-over-Launch (`orchestrator/launch/launch.go startViaLaunch`, commit `722b62ee`) — so the substrate *can* run agent-free. `InstanceConfig`/`ProcSpec` are clean. But three agent-shaped exports survive in `internal/runtime`, and two in `internal/store`, that a move would publish:

- **runtime:** `BackendDescriptor.{AgentProvisionedByBackend, AgentInstallMethod, AgentLaunchPrefix}` (runtime.go:183–185); the `AgentCommandPreparer` optional interface (runtime_optional.go:384); the `InteractiveSession`/tmux interface (runtime_optional.go:404) — the DF31 "substrate `Backend` bakes in tmux" remnant.
- **store:** `Environment.{AgentType, Model}` (environment.go:38–39) — the Q104 conflation (agent payload in the substrate record; held as plain strings so store doesn't *import* agent, but still agent *concepts*).

**Hard ordering constraint (discovered).** `internal/store/environment.go` imports `internal/runtime` (`BackendType`, `ContainerUser`). So a public `store` importing an internal `runtime` is illegal — **`runtime` must promote before, or in the same move as, `store`; `store` cannot go first.**

**The decision.** Insert a bounded **substrate surface-cleanup Shape sub-phase** before the mechanical move — each step behavior-preserving (except the one flagged), behind `internal/`, on this layering rhythm. The cleanup is shaped by the **WHAT/HOW** lens (recorded as **architecture-principles §4**, "substrate verbs accept requests and encapsulate mechanism") — *not* a blanket "extract agent fields." The runtime "agent-leak" sorts three ways:
1. **Fold the ingredient-vendors into the request verb.** `AgentLaunchPrefix`, `AgentCommandPreparer`/`PrepareAgentCommand`, and `InteractiveSession.AttachCommand` are *not* agent concepts — they are substrate HOW-fragments named "agent" by history. Each hands the caller a piece of the launch/attach mechanism to apply (`prefix + cmd`, run-the-prepared-string, exec-the-argv). Per ARCH §4 they **dissolve into `Launch`/`InteractiveExec`** and leave the public surface entirely (smaller, stabler API — not a rename). **Load-bearing, NOT cosmetic:** `AgentLaunchPrefix` is exposed *because* `lifecycle/restart.go:342` rebuilds the command from the serialized prefix "without constructing a Runtime"; folding it in means **routing restart through the backend's launch verb** — finishing the S0–S3 carve for the restart/legacy path, a real behavioral change with its own real-Docker verification. Keep `AgentProvisionedByBackend` — it is a **fact-query** (envsetup reads it to decide whether to seed), legitimately a descriptor capability. Re-home `AgentInstallMethod` (the "npm-global" value patched into `.claude.json`) to the **agent layer** — it is agent (Claude) config *payload*, neither HOW nor a substrate fact. (`InteractiveSession`'s tmux half is the DF31 session-refinement extraction.)
2. **Resolve Q104 for `store.Environment`** (the AgentType/Model conflation) — *user-call sub-question below*.
3. **Decide the store path-layout publicity** — `paths.go`'s `~/.yoloai/<sandbox>` *filename* constants (`EnvironmentFile`, `RuntimeConfigFile`, the `*.jsonl` names, …) would freeze the on-disk format as public API. Keep the filename constants unexported-as-API; expose only the path-*helper* functions (`EncodePath`, `ValidateName`, `InstanceName`, `BackendPath`, …). The layout stays an implementation detail.
4. **Stale-comment cleanup:** `InstanceConfig`'s "agent-specific fields fused here today" (runtime.go:85 — false; they're already in `orchestrator/runtimeconfig`) and `KeepaliveOnly`'s "orchestrator does not set it yet (S2c flips it)" (runtimeconfig.go — stale; S3/`722b62ee` set it).

Only then the **mechanical move**: `runtime` first/with `store`, repoint the fences (`.golangci.yml` `cli-runtime-scope` deny line 66, `cli-sandbox-scope` store deny line 98, `runtime-core-no-pty` glob line 112, + the `forbidigo` exclusions), `make releasetest` as the gate, `go vet -tags 'integration e2e'` to recompile the ~28 build-tagged test files the move touches. The `internal_leak_fence` test auto-passes (marker-based; promoted packages drop the `/internal/` marker). Procedure proven by three prior moves (copyflow/store/orchestrator lifts).

**Rejected — "move-then-clean."** Promote now with the agent-shaped fields public and remove them in a later breaking change. Rejected: it violates Frame strategy #5 ("promote a layer only when its contract has stabilized") and the no-scope-cut-on-architecture standard — a public type can't be churned the way `internal/` can, so freezing agent fields then removing them is a *guaranteed* BREAKING-CHANGES entry. The whole point of staging behind `internal/` is to do the churn *before* the semver seal.

**Out of this move (separate later promotions, confirmed not blockers).** (a) The **netpolicy** surface (`Strategy`/`CanEnforce`/`Compose`, D90) is its own refinement promotion — and `netpolicy/compose.go` still imports `agent` (`AgentFloor`, compose.go:7), an upward escaped-dep the D89 re-homing must invert (agent *provides* its domain-floor as payload) before netpolicy is DAG-clean; not gating runtime/store. (b) The **agent catalog** public surface (D89 type-vs-instance) is the agent-layer's own later promotion; its code-move is an explicit Shape task, not this move. (c) **D95 secure-secrets** credential seam is *reserved* at `EnvSpec` (envspec.go, `c292f9bd`) and sufficient — the egress-proxy build is gated on a consumer, not on the move. (d) The per-agent **detection-strategies merge gate** is satisfied (all five agents wired); it clears *main-merge*, independent of the Move. (e) Per-backend `ProcessLauncher` beyond Docker, and the public `Substrate` managed-lifecycle handle (DF32), follow the move.

**Open sub-questions (user's call) — raised alongside this entry:** (Q-A) split `store.Environment.AgentType/Model` into an agent-owned record (via `Handle.Sub`, honoring D87 "each layer persists its own facts") vs. bless them as opaque transport strings; (Q-B) do the cleanup+move on this branch as its capstone vs. merge the gate-satisfied branch to `main` now and run the Move as a fresh focused branch.

## D98 — Q104: split inside-process config (agent/model) out of the substrate record; migrate explicitly (M2)

**Date:** 2026-06-26. **Status:** Decided; scoped in [plans/store-workload-split.md](../archive/plans/store-workload-split.md); building on `substrate-move` (Stage 3b of the Move). Resolves the long-open **Q104**.

**The cut (from the user's taxonomy).** `store.Environment` (`environment.json`) mixes four kinds of fact: *constitutive* (`BackendType`, `ImageRef` — the sandbox **is** a backend instance from an image), *policy on the sandbox* (`Dirs`, `NetworkAllow`), *provenance* (`CreatedAt`, `Name`, `Principal`, `Profile` — the request, fanned out), and **inside-process config** (`AgentType`, `Model` — configuration of a *process that runs inside* the sandbox). The first three describe the sandbox; the last describes a **tenant workload**. A *substrate* record promoted to public `yoloai/store` must not describe a tenant process — that is the real Q104 conflation (not "store imports agent"; it never did — the fields are opaque strings). So `AgentType`/`Model` move to their own per-sandbox doc **`agent.json`**, **owned by orchestration** (the layer that decides what runs inside — NOT the agent *catalog*, which stays zero-import standalone); `store` provides only the persistence *mechanism* (prefer the D87 `Handle` — `agent.json` is its first real consumer).

**Migration = M2 (explicit), not M1 (transparent).** The v2→v3 step is *cross-file*: relocate `agent`/`model` out of `environment.json` into `agent.json`, reading keys the slimmed struct no longer has (a raw-JSON step — the `environment.go MigrateRecord` TODO). M1 would do this transparently at `LoadEnvironment`, but that gives `Load` a file-*write* side effect — exactly what explicit-migrate exists to avoid. **M2:** a v2 record balks with `ErrNeedsMigration`; `yoloai system migrate` gains a per-sandbox pass that relocates. Chosen because it matches the project's migration philosophy ([[feedback-migration-versioning-philosophy]] / D61: dumb plain-int checkers, no auto-migration, explicit fail-fast `system migrate` owns recognition+relocation) and the D87 direction the per-sandbox record is already TODO'd toward — and it starts retiring the legacy transparent per-sandbox auto-migrate rather than extending it.

**Build order (each its own commit; 1–2 behavior-preserving, 3 the semver cut).** (1) new `agent.json` record + orchestration ownership + `create` dual-writes it (readers still read `meta`); (2) redirect readers (`restart.go` ~10 refs, `network.go` floor, create-config, bugreport) to `agent.json`; (3) slim `store.Environment` (drop the fields, `metaVersion`→3, raw-JSON v2→v3 relocation + balk, `system migrate` per-sandbox pass); (4) tests + BREAKING-CHANGES (on-disk sandbox-record format changes; note even though `store` isn't public yet).

**Consequences.** First real consumer of the D87 `store.Handle`. `system migrate` grows a per-sandbox iteration (new). Existing sandboxes balk until migrated (deliberate, D61). `store.Environment` becomes an honest substrate-only record before the Move freezes it.

**Refinement (2026-06-26) — the split is one Move-prep pass, public home is `sb.Agent()`.** The split has a public dimension: today agent/model leak onto the public `yoloai.Environment` view. The clean surface mirrors the internal taxonomy — agent identity (type/model) lives on **`sb.Agent()`** (the agent noun, per D67/agent-layer.md), and the substrate view + public `store.Environment` carry substrate facts only. The internal slim and the public reshape (view → `sb.Agent()`) are **coupled** (can't slim the record without changing what feeds the public view), and the public reshape is deliberately Move-time. So build-order steps 2–4 + the public reshape are **resequenced into a single coherent Move-prep pass** immediately before the mechanical `git mv` — doing them mid-3b would force throwaway view-plumbing ([[feedback-feature-branch-no-transitional-scaffolding]]). Step 1 (`70e7b11f`, dual-write) pre-stages the data; 3b otherwise finishes with the trivial carve-free items. `store.Environment` still becomes agent-free before the public freeze (at Move-prep). See [plans/store-workload-split.md](../archive/plans/store-workload-split.md) §Resequencing.

## D99 — The public-layering endgame: drive to a solid, mergeable state (one clean break from main)

**Date:** 2026-06-26. **Status:** Active directive (user, 2026-06-26); the master roadmap for the rest of the `substrate-move` branch. Supersedes the piecemeal step lists (3b.x, the per-layer micro-plans) with one phased plan. Recorded in [plans/public-layering.md](../design/plans/public-layering.md) §Stages.

**The goal.** Get yoloAI into a **solid, stable shape** — clean public *and* internal interfaces, the driving consumer (control-eval) served — so the user can switch to another project with only genuinely low-priority work remaining. The whole venture lands on `main` **as one merge**.

**Two load-bearing constraints from the user.** (1) **Nothing merges to `main` until the whole venture is done**, so **incidental per-commit API/contract breakage is fine** — the branch is *one clean break* from `main`'s view; intermediate public-contract churn is free. (Each commit still compiles + passes `make check`; the "breakage" allowed is *semver/contract*, not a broken build.) This dissolves any "keep the public API stable between steps" sequencing — build straight toward the final shape; the only remaining "don't build throwaway" concern is code hygiene. (2) **80/20 API:** a small stable root (`yoloai` `Client`/`Sandbox`/`System`) resting on a more powerful layer surface (`runtime`/`store`/`copyflow`/`agent`/…) the 20% can reach directly. YAGNI, but not at the cost of things a near-term consumer needs.

**The phased roadmap.**
- **Phase 1 — seal every interface (behind `internal/`).** All breaking interface work, so the public root + to-be-promoted layers reach final shape. (1a) **session-carve public realization** [long pole]: `IOSession` on `sb.Agent()` (concentrate the ~70-file tmux scatter), the final `Launch`/`ProcSpec` contract, `AgentLaunchPrefix` off the public descriptor, slim `sandbox-setup.py` / neutral PID-1 default, **and build the one-shot `-p`/Tier-3 path** (control-eval P1/P4). (1b) **Q104** agent/model → `sb.Agent().Type()/Model()` + `agent.json` + M2 migration (now done *directly* — no view-stability dance). (1c) **`paths.go`** helpers-only. (1d) re-home the residual `entrypoint.py` secrets-read for legacy backends so the substrate owns exactly what it exports.
- **Phase 2 — near-term consumer surface (control-eval; build now, not deferred).** `yoloai wait` + MCP `sandbox_wait`; **`sandbox_run` + concurrency** (the headline unlock); `Sandbox.Usage()` token ledger; structured diff `--json`; two UX fixes (name-validation message, model in `new` summary). Independent items (wait-verb, UX) can land early for immediate value; one-shot rides 1a.
- **Phase 3 — the Move (mechanical, one consolidated break).** `git mv` the sealed layers → public — **default set `runtime`+`store`+`copyflow`+`agent`**; plumbing layers (`session`/`netpolicy`/`envsetup`) stay clean-internal for *additive-later* standalone promotion. Repoint fences, `make releasetest`, one `BREAKING-CHANGES` entry. **Final promotion set decided AT the Move**, when all interfaces are final.

**Remainder (low-priority, post-merge — none breaks a sealed interface):** Stream `SessionKind`, the D95 credential-broker build, netpolicy egress-proxy, the standalone public-package promotions of the plumbing layers, macOS-only findings (DF40), backend research (apple-container, podman+gVisor), op-hardening (log rotation, concurrency guards).

**Cross-refs:** consumer needs sourced from control-eval's trial-engine report (folded into Phase 2). Sub-plans: [session-layer.md](../design/session-layer.md) (D88, Phase 1a), [store-workload-split.md](../archive/plans/store-workload-split.md) (D98, Phase 1b), [move-audit.md](../design/plans/move-audit.md) (D97, Phase 3).

## D100 — One-shot is "headless launch in the same flow", and `yoloai run` is its sole verb

**Date:** 2026-06-26. **Status:** Active (user-converged design, 2026-06-26). Scopes Phase 1a-i. Refines [D88](#) / [session-layer.md](../design/session-layer.md) and supersedes the bare-process framing of [plans/session-carve.md](../design/plans/session-carve.md) §1a-i. Recorded in session-carve.md.

**What one-shot actually is.** Launch the agent in **its own headless mode** (`claude -p`, `gemini -p`, `aider --message …`) instead of the interactive TTY takeover. That is the *whole* delta from yoloAI's perspective — two concrete changes, nothing structural:
1. `invocation.BuildAgentCommand` takes the `HeadlessCmd` branch (prompt baked into the launch command) instead of `InteractiveCmd`. This is the time-saver: no `StartupDelay`, no ready-pattern settle, no `send-keys` prompt injection — the agent works from the first line.
2. `invocation.ResolveFallToShell` returns **off** for headless, so when the agent finishes the tmux pane **dies** (instead of dropping to a resume shell).

**Everything else is unchanged.** Same keepalive bring-up, same `sandbox-setup.py` session-runner, same tmux pane, same status monitor. Crucially, **Tier-3 (exit = done) already falls out of the existing path**: the monitor's `check_pane_dead` reads the dead pane's exit code and calls `write_status(done, exit_code)`, which `status.DetectStatus` already maps to `StatusDone`/`StatusFailed`. So `Sandbox.Wait(WaitForExit)` and `yoloai wait` observe completion **with no new launch path, no `agent-oneshot.sh` wrapper, no bare keepalive-exec, and no `status.go` change.** `sandbox-setup.py` only skips `deliver_prompt` when headless (the prompt is already in the command).

**Reconciliation with D88.** D88 imagined one-shot *shedding* tmux+monitor (agent as a bare tracked process, exit=done directly). We do **not** do that: keeping the monitor is simpler and strictly better, because pane-death **is** the done signal we'd otherwise rebuild. The earlier "no IOSession, no tmux, no monitor" language was a purity goal, not a requirement. A true zero-overhead bare-process variant remains possible later as the **Stream `SessionKind`** (post-merge remainder), if eval-at-scale ever needs it — it is explicitly *not* 1a-i.

**The CLI surface — `run` only, no `new --headless`.** Headless is meaningless without a prompt (nothing to run), and a finished headless agent leaves no live session to attach to — so every headless invocation is the run-to-completion task shape. That is exactly `run`; a `new --headless` would be a worse spelling of it. Therefore **`new` stays purely interactive (unchanged)** and **`run` is the sole headless verb**:

`yoloai run <name> [workdir] -p "…" [-d <dir>…] [--wait] [--rm]`
- **Requires a prompt** (always). **Workdir is a positional** (design intent: optional, relaxed from `new`, for the no-workdir "agent just makes API calls" case). `--dir` keeps its `new` meaning (aux dirs), never reinterpreted as the workdir. *Implementation note:* the no-workdir mode needs the create pipeline's "workdir is `Dirs[0]`" invariant broken first — deferred as **DF49**; until then `run` requires a workdir like `new`.
- **Non-blocking by default** — launch and return; the sandbox persists in `Done` for later `diff`/`apply`/`destroy`.
- **`--wait`** blocks until the agent is done (streams, exits with the agent's code).
- **`--rm` implies `--wait`**, then destroys. Fire-and-forget is the shell's job (`yoloai run … --rm &`) — **no reaper daemon** (rejected: needs infra 1a-i doesn't justify).
- **Names stay required** — no auto-generation (the "name required" safe-default holds; the earlier nameless example was a mistake).

**Library knob.** `SandboxCreateOptions.Headless bool` (create-time, persisted to runtime-config so restart reproduces it). `run` = `CreateSandbox(Headless:true)` → `Start` → optional `Wait(WaitForExit)` → optional `Destroy`. The CLI surfaces `Headless` *only* through `run`; embedders can set it directly and compose their own wait/cleanup. A terse top-level `yoloai status <name>` (scriptable poll primitive) is **deferred** — `sandbox info` covers it for now; `status` (not `state`, which collides with the workdir change-state concept) if/when added.

**Status update (D101):** the `Headless` knob is now a *preference*, not a force — create may downgrade it to interactive when the agent's headless mode would be unsafe without an API key (D101). 1a-i verified end-to-end on real Docker (success: exit 0 → done; failure: exit 3 → failed → `run` exit 1; `--rm` cleans up both). No-workdir mode deferred (DF49).

## D101 — `yoloai run` falls back to the TTY flow when headless mode would hang on auth; `--tty` forces it

**Date:** 2026-06-26. **Status:** Active (user-requested, 2026-06-26). Extends [D100](#). Backed by verified research: [research/agent-headless-auth.md](../design/research/agent-headless-auth.md).

**The problem.** Headless launch is a startup optimization, but several agents' headless modes **hang** when they lack usable auth — they try a browser/OAuth login that can never complete in a headless pane (Gemini #1696/#13853; Aider ~5 min, verified in `onboarding.py`; Codex #1569). The hang, not a worse result, is the hazard. The interactive TTY flow always works and is attachable.

**The rule (failsafe-forward; the TTY path is the always-safe fallback, only cost is a slower start).** `run` goes headless **only when the agent has authentication we can observe**, else falls back to the interactive TTY flow. With auth present an agent won't hit a login prompt, so it can't hang; without it, run interactively so the user can attach and authenticate. The gate reuses the **same `envsetup` checks as the create-time missing-auth gate** (`prepare_dirs.checkAgentAuth`) — `HasAnyAPIKey` (env keys) **||** `HasAnyAuthFile` (cred files incl. macOS Keychain) **||** `HasAnyAuthHint` (local model servers) — so there is one auth-presence convention ([[feedback-security-consistent-conventions]]). Utility agents (`test`/`idle`) declare no `APIKeyEnvVars` → `HasAnyAPIKey` true → always viable.

**Why observed-auth, not a per-agent "headless-safe" flag (the failsafe pivot, user 2026-06-26).** A flag encodes an *assumption* about an agent's headless behavior (e.g. "Claude `-p` is fine without a key") that an upstream release can invalidate — the docs even note `--bare` "will become the default for `-p`", which would flip Claude to key-only — and our code can't retroactively know. Betting on observed auth depends on nothing upstream can change: for Claude the reachable behavior is identical (create already requires Claude to have auth or it errors first), the fragile assumption is gone, and the worst case if any agent's auth stops working stays a clean failure, never a hang. The earlier `agent.Definition.HeadlessSafeWithoutAPIKey` flag was therefore **removed**. (Code guard kept: claude's `HeadlessCmd` must never add `--bare`, which is API-key-only.)

**Mechanics.** `SandboxCreateOptions.Headless` is a **preference**: create computes the *effective* headless = `requested && agentHasUsableAuth(agentDef, env, layout)`, builds the command + `fall_to_shell` from it, and **persists the effective value** to `store.Environment.Headless` (and the public `Environment.Headless`). The CLI `run` reads it back to pick its wait condition — **`WaitForExit` when headless** (pane-death = done, Tier-3), **`WaitForIdle` when the TTY fallback ran** (an interactive agent finishes its turn and goes idle rather than exiting) — and prints a one-line notice when it auto-fell-back. **`run --tty`** forces the interactive flow (sets `Headless=false`) for attach/monitor/debug; no notice (the user chose it). The downgrade is a library-internal safe default (auth lives in the library; the safe choice avoids a hang) and the effective value is observable — see [[safe-default-aligns-with-resist-defaults]].

**Accepted residual (DF50):** "credential present" ≠ "valid" — an expired token still presents a file/var, so an agent that re-auths on expiry (Gemini/Codex) could still hang headless. The durable fix is a headless launch with **no answerable interactive TTY** (a login attempt fails fast instead of stalling), tracked as **DF50** tied to the session-carve's no-TTY mode. Until then the auth-presence gate covers the common no-auth case and `--tty` is the manual escape hatch.

## D102 — split the inside-process config (agent, model) out of the substrate record (Q104, M2)

**Date:** 2026-06-26. **Status:** Active (implemented on `substrate-move`). Resolves **Q104**; chooses migration style **M2** from [store-workload-split.md](../archive/plans/store-workload-split.md).

**The decision.** `store.Environment` (`environment.json`) is the sandbox's persisted **substrate record** and is being promoted to a public layer. It carried `AgentType` + `Model` — *configuration of a process that runs inside the sandbox*, categorically distinct from the record's constitutive/policy/provenance facts. Promoting a record that also describes a tenant process's config would freeze that conflation into public semver. So agent/model move to a sibling per-sandbox doc **`agent.json`** (`internal/orchestrator/agentcfg`), owned by orchestration; `store.Environment` advances to **schema v3** and sheds the fields.

**Migration (M2 — balk + explicit `system migrate`, per [D61](#)/[[feedback-migration-versioning-philosophy]]).** `LoadEnvironment` does not migrate on read: it reads the raw `version` BEFORE unmarshalling (the slimmed struct can't see the dropped keys) and returns `ErrNeedsMigration` for any record below v3 — no write-on-read. The relocation is an explicit per-sandbox pass, `orchestrator.MigrateAgentConfigs`, wired into `System.MigrateDataDir` after `MigrateLibrary`. It is **idempotent and crash-safe**: `agent.json` (the only irreplaceable copy) is written *before* `environment.json` is rewritten, so an interrupted run loses nothing and a re-run completes. The **realm schema bumps v2 → v3** (a no-op `migrateLibraryStep`, since `internal/config` can't import store/agentcfg) purely so the existing startup gate (`RealmStatus`) prompts `yoloai system migrate` up front rather than letting the user first hit a per-sandbox balk.

**Public surface (refinement on the plan).** The substrate **view** `yoloai.Environment` sheds agent/model. They surface instead on the **aggregated read-model** `SandboxInfo` as **top-level fields** (`info.AgentType`/`info.Model`, joining `AgentStatus` — which is already top-level there, not inside `Environment`), populated from `agent.json` during inspection, AND per-handle via **`sb.Agent().Type()/Model()`**. This keeps `list` a single batched inspection (no per-row handle read) while the taxonomy holds: `Environment` = substrate facts; the aggregated Info surfaces agent identity because that is its job. Recorded in [store-workload-split.md](../archive/plans/store-workload-split.md) §"C2 build — two refinements". Mid-branch public-API inconsistency is acceptable here ([[feedback-inconsistent-public-api-ok-midbranch]]); the change lands straight to final shape, no shim.

**Accepted residual:** `prune` classifies a load error as corrupt, so a pre-v3 record with no work data could be quarantined by `prune` before migration — but the startup gate migrates everything to v3 before `prune` runs, and data-bearing dirs are detected filesystem-side (no meta needed) and refused, so no user work is at risk.

## D103 — relocate network policy off the substrate record into netpolicy.json, folded into the Q104 migration (D90)

**Date:** 2026-06-27. **Status:** Active (implemented on `substrate-move`, commit `88599217`).

**Context.** Before merging `substrate-move`, the goal was that Q104's `system migrate` (realm v2→v3 + agent.json) be the **last** on-disk migration. An audit of all deferred/post-merge work found exactly one design-intended *future* field relocation: D90's netpolicy Shape moves `NetworkMode`/`NetworkAllow` out of `environment.json` into netpolicy's own persisted record. Everything else deferred is additive `omitempty` fields, new sibling records, API-only promotions, or behavioral — none needs a migration. Rather than ship Q104's migration and then need a *second* one for netpolicy later, the relocation was folded in now.

**Decision.** Mirror D102 exactly. `NetworkMode`/`NetworkAllow` move from `store.Environment` into a sibling **`netpolicy.json`** record (`internal/netpolicycfg` — a neutral leaf, *not* under `internal/orchestrator`, so sub-orchestrator consumers like `envsetup` read it without an upward import). metaVersion stays **3** (Q104's v3 is unreleased — expand what v3 means, no v4, no second realm bump). The same per-sandbox v2→v3 pass relocates both records: `agent.json` (2a) AND `netpolicy.json` (2b) are written durably **before** the slimmed `environment.json` re-save (3) — crash-safe. Public surface follows D102: `yoloai.Environment` sheds the fields; they ride top-level on `yoloai.SandboxInfo` (from netpolicy.json at inspect). The mutable `allow`/`deny` path loads+mutates+saves the netpolicy record. Verified: extended migration tests (relocate/idempotent/crash-resume/v0-ladder + network case) + live network-isolated create on docker (netpolicy.json written; environment.json clean at v3). The deferred egress-proxy enforcement (D90/D95) reads policy from netpolicy.json and needs **no further migration** — so Q104's `system migrate` is the last one.

## D104 — retire the hand-rolled QEMU `-M microvm` backend; libkrun is the tech if a light VM tier is ever added (E1)

**Date:** 2026-06-28. **Status:** Active. **Supersedes** the E1 microvm plan ([archived](../archive/plans/microvm-backend.md)); parked branch `microvm-backend` (unmerged; spike was at `73cfe338`).

**Addendum (2026-08-05) — the spike itself is gone; this record is the artifact.** The
`microvm-backend` branch was deleted local and remote in the branch cleanup, on the owner's call,
so `73cfe338` is unreachable and will be garbage-collected. That is deliberate and costs nothing
recoverable: what the spike established is enumerated above as six sourced facts, the plan is
archived, and the revival triggers name **libkrun**, not this code — so a revival would not start
from a QEMU `-M microvm` tree that cannot boot a stock distro kernel anyway. Recorded because the
line above used to promise the branch, and a pointer to a deleted ref is worse than no pointer.

**Context.** E1 (post-merge-roadmap) was a greenfield Linux/KVM `runtime/microvm/` backend booting OCI-profile images as QEMU `-M microvm` VMs directly — the pitch was VM isolation *without* Kata's containerd/CNI/nerdctl stack. The spike and a full agent-launch increment were built (lifecycle, QGA client, OCI→ext4 conversion, baked systemd launcher, `MicrovmBackend` in sandbox-setup.py, virtiofs workdir+state shares). Real-boot validation then exposed a structural problem, and a four-thread sourced research pass reframed the whole question.

**What the spike/research established (the load-bearing facts).**
