1. **QEMU `-M microvm` cannot boot a stock distro kernel.** After the base bumped to Debian trixie's `6.12.94`, `-M microvm,acpi=on` fails the bzImage ZSTD self-decompression; `acpi=off`+PVH boots but `virtio_mmio.device=` cmdline enumeration only works with a **built-in** virtio_mmio (the distro ships it modular), so no `/dev/vda`; `pcie=on` fixes decompression but the stock kernel then hangs at ACPI init. The same kernel boots fine on **`q35`**. The reference [[reference_pve_microvm]] succeeds *only because it ships a custom-built kernel*. So `-M microvm` with a maintenance-free distro kernel is not viable — it requires owning a kernel build (exactly the burden the distro-kernel choice was meant to avoid), and `q35` is a VM, not a microVM (no millisecond boot, no minimalism).
2. **A lighter microVM adds no isolation over the Kata backend we already ship.** `--isolation vm` (Kata/QEMU) and `vm-enhanced` (Kata/Firecracker) are implemented + integration-tested today. libkrun, Firecracker, and Kata all sit on the **identical KVM hardware boundary** — a lighter microVM buys **zero** additional security. (Sources: emirb "MicroVM Isolation 2026", Edera, Northflank — all corroborating "same KVM boundary".)
3. **Boot speed is the wrong justification for our use case.** For long-lived interactive sessions, VM boot (125 ms–2 s) is a rounding error against the agent's own ~1–2 s startup and a minutes-to-hours session. Millisecond boot matters only at fleet/per-request/scale-to-zero (E2B, Lambda). (Modal: boot is "the smallest variable in the chain"; Northflank: fast boot matters "primarily for short-lived workloads".)
4. **For our threat model the higher-leverage work isn't a hypervisor.** Single-dev/own-code has no hostile co-tenant; the real risk is prompt-injection-driven destruction/exfiltration within the agent's own privileges, contained by **network egress control + no host credentials + filesystem scoping + disposability**. Anthropic ships local Claude Code with an OS-sandbox (Seatbelt/bubblewrap), *not* a VM; peer agent-runners (OpenHands, container-use) use Docker + egress allowlist. The egress proxy (D90/D95) is the higher-value security investment.
5. **Firecracker is a poor structural fit:** no virtiofs/9p/file-sharing at all (block-device only, closed/wontfix) — breaks the host-workdir copy/diff/apply model — and you maintain the guest kernel. Ruled out.
6. **libkrun is the only hand-rollable microVM that fits** (verified by a live spike): boots Debian/Alpine OCI images as KVM microVMs in ~0.4 s, **read-write virtio-fs host↔guest both directions**, multi-process works, and ships a **Red-Hat-maintained bundled kernel (`libkrunfw`)** so we never own a kernel. Integration is shell-to-`krunvm` (cgo-free, like docker/tart), and it uniquely also runs on **macOS Apple-Silicon (HVF)**.

**Decision.**
- **Retire the QEMU `-M microvm` backend.** It is dominated on every axis (kernel-maintenance trap *or* `q35` mushy-middle; duplicates Kata's isolation; no security/boot benefit for our use case). The plan is archived; the branch is parked, not merged; nothing microvm-related lands on `main`.
- **Do not add libkrun now.** It changes nothing on the isolation or boot axes that matters for us, and its honest benefits (daemon-free/easier-to-install VM tier than Kata; macOS reach) don't clear the bar today versus finishing the egress-proxy + credential-isolation work. Adoption is also currently blocked by **no Debian/Ubuntu apt package** (build-from-source only), a **macOS virtio-fs permission bug** (`root:nogroup` → needs `chmod 777`, threatens copy/diff/apply on Mac), no per-volume `:ro`, an in-progress 2.0 ABI break, and an env/cmdline `InvalidAscii` sanitization quirk.
- **If a light VM tier is ever added, it is libkrun, not QEMU-microvm or Firecracker.** Revival triggers: (a) we want *one embeddable backend* giving lightweight VM isolation across **both Linux and macOS** (libkrun's standout, complementary to the heavier Tart full-VM path); or (b) Kata's install weight becomes a real adoption complaint and a daemon-free VM tier is wanted — gated on the Debian-packaging gap closing and the macOS virtio-fs perm fix landing.

**Net.** The original "lighter than Kata" rationale survives only as install-ergonomics + macOS reach, not isolation or speed; neither clears the bar now. Kata remains the answer for "I want a VM"; the security roadmap's leverage is the egress proxy (D90/D95), credential isolation, and disposability — not a fourth VM backend.

**Addendum (2026-06-28) — libkrun vs Apple `container` on macOS.** One of libkrun's two honest benefits was "macOS reach." Apple's native `container` / Containerization framework (WWDC 2025; macOS 26 Tahoe; Apple Silicon) is the *same shape* — each Linux image in its own lightweight VM via Virtualization.framework, sub-second boot, custom Swift init (`vminitd`), optimized kernel + minimal rootfs — so on macOS there is **no isolation or boot-speed difference** vs libkrun. The secondary differences favor Apple: it is **native, Apple-maintained, and on an entrench-and-improve trajectory** (maintainers already won't fix bugs that don't reproduce on macOS 26), whereas libkrun's macOS path is third-party (`krunkit`/`krunvm`) with a known virtio-fs `root:nogroup`/`chmod 777` permission bug; both are Apple-Silicon-only. yoloAI **already ships the `apple` backend** (`runtime/apple`, `--isolation vm` on macOS 26+). So libkrun gives **no benefit over Apple `container` for macOS**; its only residual edge is *one backend across Linux+macOS* (code unification, not a macOS capability) — which competes against simply keeping the existing per-platform backends. This **knocks out libkrun's macOS-reach benefit**, leaving only the Linux "lighter-than-Kata" point already judged insufficient. The macOS bet is Apple `container` (ride Apple's investment) with Tart as the heavier full-VM fallback.

## D105 — egress proxy (workstream D): brokering is the default, containment is opt-in, phased by credential material (refines D90/D95)

**Date:** 2026-06-28. **Status:** Active (design; not yet built). **Refines** [D90](#) (netpolicy) + [D95](#) (secure-secrets) — settles the four "before D" decisions and the TLS-pinning spike, and adopts a cleaner decomposition than D90/D95 framed. Backed by this session's TLS-pinning research (all five shipped agents) + the design-doc + seam map.

**What the spike/research settled.**
1. **No agent pins certs; all SDKs support `base_url`.** Claude Code, Gemini CLI, Codex, Aider, OpenCode — none pin upstream certificates, and every underlying SDK (Anthropic/OpenAI/Google) honours a configurable `base_url` with no pinning. So **`base_url` redirect beats transparent TLS-MITM**: the agent is *configured* to talk to the proxy as its endpoint (no forged cert, no CA injection, immune to the Node/Bun/Rust CA-trust quirks). The one wrinkle: `base_url` redirects only model-API traffic (telemetry/login/update-checks/MCP still hit provider hosts), and Gemini's `base_url` is honoured only under API-key auth.
2. **The design conflated two separable concerns.** Pull them apart:
   - **Credential protection** (the key never enters the sandbox) needs only `base_url` + a **fixed-upstream injector**. It is *cheap* and safe even without forcing traffic — the key is safe because the agent doesn't have it, not because traffic is forced; the injector always forwards to the real upstream regardless of what the agent asks (unforgeable reach, the IMDSv2 property).
   - **Egress restriction** (allowlist) needs the heavier **default-deny netns + forced forwarding**.

**Decisions.**
- **(a) Injection transport = `base_url` redirect**, not transparent MITM. MITM-with-injected-CA is kept only as a *deferred fallback* for non-redirectable injection (and the per-agent CA knobs — `NODE_USE_SYSTEM_CA`/`NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, `CODEX_CA_CERTIFICATE`, Aider `SSL_CERT_FILE`, Bun caveats — are documented for that path).
- **(b) Brokering the agent's own API key is the DEFAULT, always-on.** This dissolves D95's open "trust-posture: when does brokering engage" question *for the agent's API key* — it always does. Egress restriction stays **opt-in and open by default** (unchanged from today). Net default posture: **"API keys brokered; egress open unless restricted."** Brokering protects the *key*, not arbitrary data exfil — open-egress still lets a duped agent leak data to reachable hosts; that is the opt-in containment layer's job, stated honestly so the default isn't oversold.
- **(c) Two-layer architecture.** (1) **Always-on key-injector** — a small fixed-upstream reverse proxy for just the LLM endpoint; general traffic goes *direct* (no forwarder, no default-deny). (2) **Opt-in egress containment** — default-deny netns + an SNI-splicing forwarder (reads plaintext SNI, allow/deny, splices bytes — no decryption) + allowlist; when enabled it subsumes the injector (one component). The injector must be in every session's hot path reliably → needs a `--no-broker`/direct escape hatch (rootless hosts, debugging).
- **(d) Proxy tech = bespoke small Go** (stdlib reverse-proxy + SNI splice; goproxy-style CA-MITM only if/when the MITM fallback is built). **Rejected:** mitmproxy (Python runtime dep), LiteLLM/Portkey (Python service / LLM-gateway-opinionated), Cloudflare AI Gateway (hosted SaaS → credentials leave the host, breaks the containment model). The user's instinct "don't take on proxying" is honoured by keeping the always-on piece tiny — a fixed-upstream injector, not an all-traffic proxy.
- **(e) Enforcement scope = Linux-first.** docker/podman/Kata/gVisor via **host-side nftables on the veth** (enforced outside the agent's reach; uniquely gives **gVisor** a real allowlist for the first time — its userspace netstack ignores in-sandbox iptables). macOS deferred: Tart/Apple via host `pf` on the TAP (unwired today, `NetworkIsolation:false`); **Seatbelt has no netns/veth to filter** (hardest, may be unsupportable for `isolated`).
- **(f) Phase by credential material.**
  - **Phase 1 — broker metered API keys** (`sk-ant-api…` + static/`ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` shapes). Clean: proxy holds it, injects `x-api-key`, agent has no key. This is the default for API-key auth. Detection reuses the agent layer's existing auth signals (`HasAnyAPIKey`/`HasAnyAuthFile`).
  - **Phase 2 — broker subscription OAuth** (`/login` Pro/Max). The case that *most* needs brokering (short-lived `sk-ant-oat` + refresh is incompatible with env/file delivery) and whose today's path is the *worst* exfil target (the long-lived **refresh token** sits in `~/.claude/.credentials.json` in the box). Mechanism: user logs in **once on the host**; the proxy holds the host-side refresh token (caller-injected `CredentialSource`), mints/refreshes the `oat`, injects `Authorization: Bearer`; the sandbox agent gets `base_url` + a stable **dummy bearer handle** so it never `/login`s and holds no real credential (not even the refresh token). Cost: the proxy owns Anthropic's OAuth refresh loop.
  - **Later** — Bedrock/Vertex (cloud request-signing), the MITM fallback, and the opt-in egress-containment layer.
- **(g) Direct delivery (today's file/env path) is RETAINED as the per-backend transitional fallback — no flag-day.** Brokering rolls out backend-by-backend; until a backend is migrated (and until Phase 2 for subscription on *any* backend), credentials fall back to direct delivery. The honest residual: subscription auth keeps its refresh token in the box until brokered, whereas metered keys are protected from day one.
- **(h) Keep the broker GENERAL — not Claude/LLM-specific.** The `CredentialSource` + destination/header credential-shape must support brokering credentials for **any** auth'd tool the agent uses — **git** (a host-side GitHub App minting 1h repo-scoped tokens via a credential helper, per D95's existing git-broker note), package registries, other LLM providers. Reserve the capability in the seam now; don't bake an LLM-only shape into `EnvSpec`/`CredentialSource`.

**Refined build order (supersedes roadmap §D's "TLS-MITM first" order).** (1) `CredentialSource` + general `EnvSpec` credential-shape (destination/header/CA-knob — tool-agnostic). (2) Always-on key-injector → metered (the default). (3) Subscription-OAuth broker (refresh loop). (4) Opt-in egress containment (Linux nftables + SNI forwarder + allowlist; strategy-dispatch `LivePatchNetwork`). (5, later) MITM fallback for non-redirectable injection; macOS enforcement; Bedrock/Vertex; git broker.

**Spike items to confirm before/at build:** Claude Code with `base_url` + dummy bearer does not fall back to interactive `/login`; subscription-through-broker sits within Anthropic's terms (still Claude Code calling, creds injected); Bun CA-var reliability *if* the MITM fallback is exercised. The honest residuals from D95 (brokering bounds blast radius / makes exfil scoped + detectable — it does not stop a duped agent misusing an in-scope capability; compose with the copy/diff/apply review gate + a narrow allowlist + egress logging) carry forward unchanged.

**Validation addendum (2026-06-28) — research + live spike confirm the design; the broker shape gains one reservation.** Three research threads + a hands-on spike (recorded under [research/egress-broker-spike/](../design/research/egress-broker-spike/); build brief in [plans/egress-proxy-build.md](../design/plans/egress-proxy-build.md)).

- **Live spike (Claude Code, real binary, no real creds):** with `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL=http://127.0.0.1:PORT` + `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN=dummy` + a pre-seeded `~/.claude.json` `{"hasCompletedOnboarding":true}`, Claude POSTed `/v1/messages?beta=true` to the mock with `Authorization: Bearer dummy-broker-token-123`, rendered the mock's canned SSE, exited 0, **no `/login` fallback.** The exact injection seam (agent sends a placeholder; proxy swaps in the real key) is proven end-to-end.
- **All five agents are brokerable via `base_url` + a dummy credential** (plain `http://` localhost accepted by all; no cert-pinning blockers), each with per-agent launch knobs that fit the existing agent-definition pattern:
  | Agent | Knobs | Caveat |
  |---|---|---|
  | Claude | `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` + `ANTHROPIC_AUTH_TOKEN` (precedence #2, verbatim Bearer) | pre-seed `hasCompletedOnboarding:true` or onboarding bypasses base_url to `api.anthropic.com` |
  | Codex | custom `[model_providers.X] base_url` + `env_key` + `forced_login_method="api"` | proxy must speak the **Responses API** (`/responses`); avoid the built-in `openai` provider |
  | Gemini | `GOOGLE_GEMINI_BASE_URL` + `GEMINI_API_KEY=dummy` | base_url honoured **only in API-key mode**; OAuth ignores it → fixed `cloudcode-pa.googleapis.com` |
  | Aider | `--openai-api-base` + `--openai-api-key dummy` | model name **must** be `openai/<model>` or litellm routes to the real host |
  | OpenCode | provider `options.baseURL` + `options.apiKey` | runtime `npm i` of the provider pkg + `models.dev` fetch fire **before** the proxy — pre-cache in the image; Bun `fetch()` ignores `HTTP(S)_PROXY` |
- **Generalized broker shape — validated against git/npm/pip/Docker/AWS/GCP/Azure; gains one required reservation.** The D105 `{destination, header-template, CredentialSource}` covers static bearer/basic (git/npm/PyPI/Azure-api-key) and refreshable bearer (GCP/Vertex/Azure-Entra/GitHub-App), but **breaks on two classes**: request-signing (AWS SigV4, Azure SharedKey) and multi-request token-exchange (Docker/OCI). Reserve the richer shape now (retrofitting it later is a breaking interface change):
  ```
  CredentialBinding { Destination, Apply, Source }
    Apply  = header-set | basic-auth | request-signer   // closed 3-variant enum
    Source = static | refreshing(Fetch->value,expiresAt) | minting
  ```
  `request-signer` (aws-sigv4 | azure-sharedkey) is a transform over the **final** request and **must run last** — implement `header-set`/`basic-auth` first but reserve the signer variant. Docker/OCI is **not** solved in the proxy: route it to a `minting` source feeding an **in-sandbox credential helper** (the client runs its own 401->exchange->Bearer dance). `expiresAt` already covers GCP/Azure refresh, the GitHub-App 1h token, and the ~5-min OCI token.
- **Subscription (Phase 2) de-risked:** prefer the **sanctioned** `claude setup-token` -> 1-year `CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN` (held host-side, injected) over reverse-engineering Anthropic's OAuth refresh (undocumented endpoint/client_id, refresh-token rotation, discouraged for third parties — fragile).
- **Architectural notes confirmed:** the injector stays simple — a per-sandbox forward-and-swap reverse proxy to the agent's configured real upstream; the per-agent complexity (base_url knob, force-API-key-mode for Gemini/Codex, the `openai/` prefix, telemetry/update-suppression flags, OpenCode npm pre-cache) lives in **launch config**, not the proxy. Wire-protocol divergence (Codex=Responses, others=Chat/Messages/`generativelanguage`) is handled by per-agent upstream config, not a translator.
- **Build-time confirmation gaps (each ~30-min localhost echo):** Codex zero-key-validation + exact Responses request; Gemini's current base_url env-var spelling (version churn); OpenCode `options`-forwarding on the pinned version (#5674).

## D106 — egress proxy: the key-injector's lifetime is a pluggable host (CLI sidecar / embedder in-process), recovery is lazy re-derivation (refines D105)

**Date:** 2026-06-28. **Status:** Active (building). **Refines** [D105](#d105--egress-proxy-workstream-d-brokering-is-the-default-containment-is-opt-in-phased-by-credential-material-refines-d90d95). Grounded in a live host-side spike (real Dockerized Claude through a host-side `broker.Injector` via `host.docker.internal` — placeholder swapped for the real key, no `/login`, exit 0) plus a code audit of the session/credential model. Settles **who owns the always-on injector's lifetime**, which D105 left open.

**The forcing facts.**
1. **The session is detached by default.** `yoloai run` returns once the agent is launched (run.go); the agent runs *inside the container* (headless `-p` or a tmux pane) while the **CLI process exits** and the sandbox persists `StatusActive`. So an in-process, Engine-owned injector goroutine would die under a still-running agent — wrong model for the CLI.
2. **Injector liveness is inseparable from credential liveness.** The injector must outlive the CLI, and on restart it needs the credential again. Where that credential comes from differs by entry point, and that difference *is* the ownership axis (fact 3).
3. **The CLI credential is an ambient read; the embedder's is in-process state.** For the CLI the key is captured by the licensed `os.Environ()` read at the boundary (`host_env.go` `WithEnv`, `EnvForAgentCredentials`) — a shell var or host auth file whose source of truth **outlives any sidecar and is re-derivable by any later invocation** (it even picks up rotation). For the library embedder the credential is a programmatic value / `CredentialSource` `Fetch` hook passed through `yoloai.NewClient` — it lives in *that process's memory* and **cannot be reconstructed by a separate process**.

**Decisions.**
- **(a) Pluggable injector host.** Define an `InjectorHost` seam owning a sandbox's injector lifetime. Two implementations: **CLI → out-of-process sidecar** (survives the CLI; re-derives the key on respawn) and **embedder → in-process host** (holds the live `CredentialSource`). The injector itself (`broker.Injector`, D105/2a) is unchanged; only its *host* varies.
- **(b) Phase 1 = CLI / sidecar / static keys only.** Refreshing & minting sources are **gated to the in-process host** — we do not fake recovery we cannot perform. This matches the re-derivability split exactly: static/ambient → sidecar; programmatic/refresh → in-process.
- **(c) Recovery is lazy re-derivation — no secret is ever persisted.** A dead sidecar is the rare exception; the loud signal is the agent's API calls failing (502/refused). Recovery: any CLI command **that targets a single named sandbox** (`status <name>`, `attach <name>`, `diff <name>`, `restart <name>`…) reconciles — liveness-checks the recorded PID and respawns by **re-deriving** the key from the current ambient env. **Multi-sandbox commands (`ls`/`ps`/`prune`) never reconcile** (no fan-out respawn). The residual gap — a sidecar that dies during a long session with *no* CLI activity stays dead until the next single-sandbox touch — is acceptable for Phase 1 and is the natural job of a **future daemon** (which also becomes the in-process host for embedder/refresh sources). The "persist a 0600 key on disk for auto-respawn" option was considered and **rejected**: for the CLI the key is already re-derivable, so persistence buys nothing and only widens the at-rest surface.
- **(d) Two security defaults for the sidecar.** The real key crosses parent→child via the child's **stdin** (a config JSON), never argv (ps-visible) nor the persisted record. The sidecar binds to the **container-reachable host interface** (the bridge gateway, e.g. `172.17.0.1`), **not `0.0.0.0`** — so it is not exposed on the LAN. Residual: other containers on the same bridge can reach it (the injector forwards for any caller) — that is the opt-in containment layer's job, stated honestly per D105 ("brokering protects the key, not arbitrary egress").

**Build decomposition (workstream D, step 2b).** **2b-1** — the host machinery in `internal/broker`, package-only and Docker-free-testable: a `RunSidecar` entry (config+secret via stdin, handshake the resolved listen addr back via stdout, serve), the `InjectorHost` interface + `SidecarHost` (spawn detached via `Setsid`, persist `injector.json` = PID + addr, reap, `Ensure`/reconcile by PID-liveness), tested against a mock upstream and a real spawned subprocess (test-helper-process pattern). **2b-2** — wire it into the launch path (start after `buildAndStart`, reap before `Runtime.Stop`/teardown), register the hidden `__inject` subcommand, add the per-agent base_url/dummy/seed knobs (Claude first), perform the credential split (real key → sidecar, dummy+`base_url` → container, real key omitted from `ProcSpec.Env`), `--no-broker` + direct-delivery fallback (no flag-day), reconcile triggers on the single-sandbox commands, and the real-Claude Docker integration test (relocating the spike's `mock-anthropic.py` into the package `testdata/`).

**Residuals carried forward (unchanged from D105).** Brokering bounds blast radius and makes exfil scoped + detectable; it does not stop a duped agent misusing an in-scope capability — compose with the copy/diff/apply review gate, a narrow allowlist, and egress logging. PID-liveness can be fooled by PID reuse (a false "alive"); the symptom is the same failing API call → harder reconcile/`restart`; a holder-lock refinement is deferred until it bites.

## D107 — Clever hacks bite: lean on a foreign system's *contract*, not an incidental property (new principle GEN §14, bounds GEN §3)

**Date:** 2026-06-29. **Status:** Active. Establishes a new general principle (GEN §14) and draws the boundary on GEN §3 (ecosystem-first). Grounded in the v0.6.0 docker base-image staleness fix (`e9837be8`).

**The principle.** Repurposing an existing system to *incidentally* serve a use it was not designed for is brittle: the system wasn't shaped around our case and won't concern itself with changes that break us. A hack needs very strong justification — "it's clever" is the worst smell of all. Canonical illustrations (not ours): Go makes an identifier public by an *uppercase first letter*, which has no meaning in caseless scripts (forcing the ugly `X成本` workaround); Go marks test code by the `_test.go` *filename*, so sharing test helpers across packages forces them out into public `testutil`/`pkgtest` surface.

**Boundary with GEN §3.** §3 says compose with git/Docker/unix rather than reinventing. §14 is its edge: compose with what a tool *contractually* provides, not with an incidental property it happens to expose. Not a tension — §3 picks the tool, §14 picks *which surface of it* you lean on.

**The worked case (ours).** The docker base image could go stale on a second local provider: freshness was tracked by a host-side marker file keyed by *backend* (`.base-image-checksum-docker`), but OrbStack / Docker Desktop / Colima are separate image stores under one "docker" backend (the provider-dimension sibling of DF56). **Rejected fix (the hack):** key the marker by the daemon's `docker info .ID`. It worked for Docker but leaned on a property that is *empty* for podman's docker-compat API — so correctness silently varied by daemon. Exactly the smell: a side-channel (host file) patched with a foreign incidental (daemon ID) that doesn't serve all daemons. **Root fix:** stamp the checksum as a **label on the image itself**, read back via `ImageInspect` → `Config.Labels`. Freshness travels with the artifact, in whatever store holds it — no host-side key, no daemon property, immune to the provider/symlink/remote dimensions by construction. The single-store backends (apple — a Tart VM image, not OCI; containerd — one store) correctly keep the host marker.

**Consequences.** GEN §14 added with this case as its worked example; `rule-index.md` gains a GEN §14 row; the principles `README.md` general index line updated. The backend-idiosyncrasies entry for the docker staleness bug records the rejected daemon-ID approach and why.

## D108 — Copy-mode work-copy git runs in the agent's confinement, not on the host (closes audit C1 / DF66)

**Date:** 2026-06-29. **Status:** Active. Closes the sole CRITICAL blocking the v0.6.0 tag (DF66) for the container backends. Applies GEN §14 (D107): put the *execution* where it belongs.

**The decision.** The `:copy` work copy's `.git/config` + `.gitattributes` are agent-controlled (RW bind mount), and git executes shell from repo-local config — `filter.*.clean/smudge`, `diff.*.command/textconv`, `core.fsmonitor` — not only from `.git/hooks`. Running that git on the **host** (for `yoloai diff`/`apply`/`status`) is host code execution by a prompt-injected agent. A correct diff of a filtered repo *must* run the filters (Git LFS, git-crypt, redaction), so the only option that is both secure **and** correct is to run the work-copy git **inside the same confinement the agent already runs in** — exactly where Tart's `SandboxSide` git already ran. The host-side "private git-dir + clean config" alternative is rejected: it silently corrupts every filtered repo (committed blob cleaned, work tree read raw → wrong, secret-leaking patches).

**Why this is the §14 move, not a hack.** git's *contract* is that diff normalizes the work tree *through* the configured filters. Any host-side fix that stays secure must subvert that contract (starve git of config) — the brittle incidental-property hack §14 warns against, breaking the moment a repo uses filters as intended. The contractual move: let git do its whole job, and place the *isolation* on the boundary we own (the agent's confinement). §14's "put the data where it belongs" reads here as **put the execution where it belongs**.

**How it shipped.** New predicate `runtime.GitRunsInConfinement(rt)` = `SandboxSide` ∪ `BackendCaps.GitExecInConfinement` (set by docker/podman/containerd), decoupling *git-exec locality* from *FilesystemLocality* (work copies stay host-readable for docker; only git execution moves). `git.NewSandbox` dispatches the work-copy git through each backend's `GitExec(ctx, instance, user, workDir, …)`; `git.sandboxExec` (allowed to import `store`) maps the host work-copy path → the in-sandbox mount path and resolves the instance + agent container user, keeping `runtime` decoupled from `store` and every copyflow/orchestrator call site unchanged (they still pass the host path). The host-**target** apply path (`git apply`/`am` into the user's real repo) keeps `NewHost` — already sound. Create/reset baselining stays host-side (runs before the agent, on a clean `.git`).

**Consequences.** Copy-mode `diff`/`apply` now require the sandbox running (clear "start the sandbox" error at the public `Workdir` boundary, `errors.Is(ErrNotRunning)` preserved; `status`/`list` degrade to "unknown" via `workprobe`) — same UX shape as `:overlay` and as Tart already had. Verified on real Docker + Podman (malicious-filter containment + legit-filter correctness). Residuals in **DF67**: seatbelt (no container — host git under `sandbox-exec`, macOS-only, needs caps-F5) and the broken-metadata `ProbeWorkData` host-git fallback. Full design + as-shipped deviations: [plans/copy-mode-git-rce.md](../archive/plans/copy-mode-git-rce.md).

## D109 — Retire `:overlay`; base `:copy` on reflink (copy-on-write) copies. Supersedes the H2/DF65 "gate `:overlay`" direction

**Date:** 2026-06-29. **Status:** Active. Supersedes the gate-`:overlay` recommendation in the H2 audit (DF65 / [overlay-sysadmin-escape.md](../archive/plans/overlay-sysadmin-escape.md)). Plan: [retire-overlay-reflink-copy.md](../design/plans/retire-overlay-reflink-copy.md).

**The decision.** Remove the `:overlay` mount mode and recover its benefit (instant setup / low disk for large working trees) by making `:copy` use copy-on-write clones (FICLONE on Linux, clonefile on macOS) with transparent fallback to a full copy.

**Why drop, not gate.** `:overlay`'s only edge over `:copy` is skipping the upfront copy via an in-container **kernel overlayfs**, which forces `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` + `apparmor=unconfined`. On rootful Docker (the common, non-userns-remapped case) that is a host escape (`cgroup release_agent`/`core_pattern`), and the H2 audit proved it is **not cheaply fixable** — fuse-overlayfs needs the same cap outside a user namespace (GEN §14 trap). The structural problem: `:overlay` is safe only where it's least needed (Podman rootless, Apple VM, userns-remapped Docker) and a host escape where it's most common, and it's incompatible with gVisor — so it pulls users toward the weakest isolation tier. **Audience collapse:** the only operators who can run `:overlay` safely are exactly those sophisticated enough to put `~/.yoloai` on a copy-on-write filesystem, where a reflink `:copy` delivers the *same* instant/low-disk benefit with no privilege, full snapshot isolation, every backend, offline diff/apply, and none of the VirtioFS/nested-overlay fragility. So the safe audience doesn't need overlay and the needy audience (rootful Docker) can't use it safely.

**Reflink was verified before deciding** (empirical, not assumed): copy-on-write clone is safe to depend on across btrfs, XFS (`reflink=1`, the `mkfs.xfs` default since xfsprogs 5.1), and APFS — it clones with shared blocks, is **CoW-independent** (editing the clone never touches the original; safe to `git diff`), and **falls back silently to a full copy** on every non-CoW / cross-filesystem case (ext4, old-XFS, ZFS-default, NFS, overlayfs, WSL DrvFs, cross-device, EXDEV). Documented idiosyncrasies don't break correctness: the **OpenZFS 2.2.0 block-cloning corruption** was a pre-existing `dnode_is_dirty` bug *fixed in 2.2.2*, with block cloning **disabled by default through 2.2.6** (so FICLONE falls back to a full copy on default ZFS); **btrfs `defrag` un-sharing** reflinked extents is disk-accounting only. The fallback-only (`=auto`) discipline neutralizes all of them. One non-correctness caveat: the speed/disk win only materializes when the project and `~/.yoloai` share a filesystem (macOS satisfies this by default — single APFS Data volume).

**Consequences.** Two-phase, replacement-before-removal: **Phase 1** makes `:copy` reflink-aware (additive, no migration, ships first — note the *default* gitignore `:copy` path currently never clones, and Linux has no clone at all, so this is real work in `internal/workspace/copy.go`'s `copyFile` + a new `copy_linux.go` FICLONE); **Phase 2** removes `:overlay` (breaking, next minor, ~18 files + a parallel diff/apply/baseline path + `entrypoint.py apply_overlays` + the `OverlayDirs` capability + the `SYS_ADMIN`/apparmor grant; needs a fail-fast `system migrate` for existing overlay sandboxes and a BREAKING-CHANGES entry). Until Phase 2 lands, the v0.6.0 interim stands: `:overlay` is a documented dangerous opt-in with a loud warning. Open questions (migration policy, Phase 2 timing, same-FS ergonomics) are tracked in the plan.

## D110 — Crash-safe migration model (linear data-dir schema chain + staging-workdir/atomic-rename, decoupled from release cadence)

**Date:** 2026-06-30. **Status:** Active — **design open for critique, not yet built.** Plan: [crash-safe-migration.md](../archive/crash-safe-migration.md); audit: [crash-safe-migration-audit.md](../archive/crash-safe-migration-audit.md). Addresses DF68; prerequisite for D109's overlay flatten.

**The model.** A migration *run* is an ordered set of **discrete, per-realm, individually-versioned** migrations. Each stages into a `migration-<build-id>/` workdir, transforms its unit, and commits via a **resumable atomic rename** (commit state encoded in filenames — no separate write-ahead journal); the displaced original is transient (deleted on completion — migration is one-way, no retained backup). **One shape — build-new → repopulate (move unchanged) → atomic-rename swap** — applied per *unit* (a realm = one unit, all-or-nothing; sandboxes = many, done one at a time, each committed independently, quarantine-or-abort on failure). The swap commits **all of a unit's changed files atomically — including the new `.schema-version`, which lives inside the unit** (no separate flip; every migration changes ≥2 files; C3 gone structurally). During promotion `_^^_orig` keeps the old version (derives the move-list) and `_^^_new` the new; recovery reads the live version to place itself in the chain. **Refinements (2026-06-30, supersedes the fusion/sub-realm framing).** Migrations are a **single linear data-dir schema chain** (v2→v3→v4→…), **decoupled from release numbers** — a migration ships when ready; only schema-step order matters. A migration may reach into sandboxes via an **idempotent per-sandbox pass** (the v2→v3 split's existing pattern); per-sandbox progress is keyed on **observable on-disk form, not a per-sandbox version file** (the sandbox-as-sub-realm idea is **dropped**). A *unit* (build-new→swap target) is the data-dir realm or a single sandbox during the pass. **No fusion:** the existing agent.json split stays **library v2→v3, sealed as-is** (not reworked); the **overlay→copy flatten is the next migration, v3→v4** — the first customer of the crash-safe machinery (seed a copy dir from the lower + apply the upper onto it; both platforms require the sandbox *running* per DF69 / a stopped overlay sandbox is a plan-surfaced choice). The single data-dir `.schema-version` is **flipped last** (after the per-sandbox pass + fsync barrier). `*_^^_orig` is **moved to `trash/`, not deleted**. **fsync kept** for power-loss recoverability. Scratch is a single disposable top-level dir (cleared between chain steps). **Overlay removal** rides the same v3→v4 release (decision 8, resolved below): delete all overlay create/start code + `:overlay` as a creatable mode, keep only the read-glue — so no separate post-removal binary and no detect-and-refuse. The sealed v2→v3 split is **left as-is** (retro-harden A4/DF68 is a deferred small fix, not in scope). **Truth invariant (the DF68 fix):** each sandbox's on-disk form is the source of truth; the data-dir stamp is a cache **flipped last**, only after a durable barrier confirms every sandbox is at ≥ target (or quarantined) — the stamp is physically incapable of being ahead of the data. **Separation:** generic machinery owns the workdir lifecycle, the seed-copy (faithful reproduction + special-file/disk handling, once), the durable-atomic-rename primitive, the resumable promotion + recovery, and backup GC; each migrator owns the transform and **source consistency** (start/stop containers, avoid torn reads, and *detect-and-refuse* an already-destroyed source). Supersedes the first-cut WAL-journal/snapshot-rollback design.

**Why (the audit).** Four independent adversarial reviewers (A1–A18) + in-code verification. The spine **dissolves** the most error-prone pieces — the WAL journal (A8), DF68 stamp-before-pass, the CLI-realm-outside-the-substrate bug (A6), and in-place flatten (A9) — and **reduces** the snapshot disk/quiescence cost (A7) to one sandbox at a time. Four claims were verified in code: there is **no durable/atomic write primitive** today (A1 — every commit/stamp is bare `os.WriteFile`), the existing agent.json split is **already power-loss-lossy** (A4/C3 — values-first but unfsynced), the per-sandbox flock **does not imply quiescence** (A5 — released once the container launches), and a successful migration is a **downgrade ratchet** (R1 — `RealmStatus` hard-errors an older binary). The macOS overlay flatten has a **silent-total-loss** path (A2/DF69) the copy model can't dissolve (tmpfs source, nothing on disk to copy) — the migrator must refuse a stopped macOS overlay sandbox.

**Migration chain (decoupled from release cadence, 2026-06-30).** A linear schema chain, not pinned to releases. **v2→v3** = agent.json split — existing, shipping, **sealed as-is** (carries the A4/DF68 power-loss exposure, mitigated by idempotent re-run; retro-hardening is a separate open call). **v3→v4** = overlay→copy flatten — the **first customer of the crash-safe machinery** (build-new→swap + fsync + resumable rename + whole-tree lock); per-sandbox pass, seed-from-lower + apply-upper, macOS requires running (DF69). **overlay removal** rides the same v3→v4 release: delete all overlay create/start code + `:overlay` as a creatable mode, keep only the read-glue (no mount code) — so no separate post-removal binary, no detect-and-refuse, no ordering constraint (decision 8 resolved). The machinery lands with v3→v4, where the destructive flatten both needs and exercises it. Verified in code: the split is the v2→v3 library migration (`MigrateAgentConfigs`, run via `system migrate`, gate-enforced, bare `os.WriteFile`), reachable on `release-prep`.

**Final shape (2026-06-30) — rejected the general carve model; sandboxes are a one-off.** A recursive sub-realm/carve framework (any dir gains a `.schema-version` to become its own realm; leaf-first traversal; cross-realm staging for hoists) was explored and **rejected as brittle** — the cross-realm linkages made it fragile, and shoehorning the sandbox special-case into a grand model wasn't worth it. Settled model: **two independent realm chains, `library` and `cli`, each with one linear `.schema-version`** that orders its own migrations; they migrate independently — with **one structural exception**: the `MigrateCLI` flat→namespaced relocation creates `library/` and lifts library-owned content into it, so on a flat-v0 install the cli realm must relocate before the library realm can see its contents (`system migrate` honors this: cli first, then re-reads library status — `migrate.go:57-77`) — but **both must be current before yoloai runs** (the existing gate). Because the `v3→v4` overlay flatten's `Plan()` runs *before* any relocation, the flatten migrator (orchestrator-resident — it needs `Exec`/`ApplyOverlay` types `internal/config` can't import — so it legitimately spans realms) resolves its sandboxes root as **`library/sandboxes/` if present, else top-level `sandboxes/`**, so the plan is accurate on a not-yet-migrated install; this covers un-migrated and fully-migrated but **not** a half-relocated install (audit A6 — stranded top-level overlay sandboxes silently skipped; the pre-existing exposure of the sealed flat→namespaced migration, not a new defect). **No top-level `$YOLOAI_HOME` marker** (decided 2026-06-30, no): its direct children are just whatever is installed (library always; cli/daemon optionally), and the one above-realm migration we have — the `MigrateCLI` flat→namespaced relocation — stays as-is; a root realm wasn't worth the uniformity. **Schema versions are decoupled from app versions** → a release can carry several migrations, and one migration can bundle several changes. **This branch = a single `v3→v4` bump** covering overlay retirement + any other on-disk change made here; the overlay flatten is a per-sandbox pass (each sandbox converted in isolation via build-new→swap, **no per-sandbox marker** — progress read from on-disk form). **Bespoke migrators, lean framework (no cathedral), no mid-run prompts:** interaction is centralized via **plan → confirm → apply** (terraform-style). Each migrator exposes `Plan()` (read-only: describe ops, each flagged *destructive*, plus foreseen refusals/quarantines) + `Apply()` (the transform, re-validating preconditions at apply time). The **app** renders the plan and takes the **one** confirmation (`--yes` to proceed; **default-abort** headless/no-TTY on any destructive op); apply is gated on a **policy flag**, not a passed-in (possibly stale) plan object; the **library never prompts**. **All-or-nothing is the *decision*, not the *execution*** (each unit still commits incrementally + resumably; no transactional rollback — no-downgrade forbids it). This **resolves the bad-sandbox policy (A12)**: quarantines are surfaced in the plan and approved-or-aborted with the whole plan. `migrate --dry-run`/`--check` prints the plan (`--json`) and is the pre-upgrade audit (A15). Migration *policy* lives in the app, migration *responsibility* (durable staging + atomic-swap + lock + recovery) in the framework; cross-migrator dependencies are **non-uniform and that's accepted**. The v2→v3 split is **left as-is** ("the agent.json ship has sailed" — A4/DF68 exposure stays, mitigated by idempotent re-run). **Overlay reader resolution (decision 8, 2026-06-30; verified in code):** flattening an **already-running** overlay sandbox needs **zero overlay runtime/mount code** — only generic `Exec` (git in the live container) + host-side `git apply` + a workdir copy + two constants (merged path + the baseline SHA in `environment.json`). So the new binary **deletes all overlay create/start code** (entrypoint mount, `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` grant + AppArmor/seccomp/podman-userns exceptions, `mounts.go` overlay specs, `collectOverlayMounts`, `OverlayMountConfig`, dir init) + `:overlay` as a creatable mode, and **keeps only the small read-glue** (`generateOverlayPatchForContext`/`ApplyOverlay`/baseline helpers), which execs into a container a *prior* binary already mounted. Consequences: **dissolves detect-and-refuse + stepping-stone for overlay** (A13/A14); **no two-binary ordering constraint** (flatten + removal share a release); **maximal security win** — the new binary **cannot mount overlayfs at all** (no `SYS_ADMIN`/mount code), it only *reads* from an already-mounted container (flagged for security review). Cost/precondition: **requires the sandbox already running** (the binary can't start a stopped overlay), **both platforms**; a **stopped** overlay sandbox is a plan-surfaced choice — go back/downgrade/start/re-upgrade/re-run (preserves changes), or proceed destructively (abandons the overlay changes: macOS already gone per DF69; Linux displaced upper → `trash/`, manually recoverable). Supersedes the linear-chain-with-per-sandbox-pass framing's open questions, the never-written carve model, *and* the earlier split-reader-from-detector retirement plan.

**Re-audit revision (2026-06-30, supersedes the flatten *mechanic* above — the model, chain, plan/apply, and security conclusion stand).** A second four-reviewer adversarial audit (verified in code) retired the **seed-from-lower + in-container-`git diff` + host-`git apply`** flatten mechanic that the decision-8 / Source-consistency / chain paragraphs above describe. That mechanic (a) silently dropped agent work — a lazy/empty baseline yields an empty diff → flatten = the original workdir with all changes lost (re-audit **B1**, CRITICAL, code-verified); double-applied when the workdir had uncommitted changes (**B2**); and dropped gitignored upper files, permanently on macOS (**B3**); and (b) rode the **DF70** host-`git apply --unsafe-paths` traversal hole. **Revised flatten: a raw recursive copy of the merged tree** (`cp -a`/`cp -rp`, the existing primitive) into the new `:copy` work dir — **no git, no tar, no baseline diff** — destination-confined for free (a dir entry can't be named `..`). This **dissolves B1/B2/B3** (the merged tree *is* what `:copy` holds, gitignored + uncommitted included) and keeps the flatten clear of both the C1 filter/hook class and DF70. Consequently decision 8 is **strengthened**: the new binary **deletes *all* overlay code including the git read-glue** (`generateOverlayPatchForContext`/`ApplyOverlay`/baseline helpers), keeping only path-location knowledge — not "keeps only the read-glue" as written above. **DF70** (host `git apply --unsafe-paths` of a container-generated patch = arbitrary host-file write; the `--unsafe-paths` is only needed because the apply runs from a temp repo elsewhere + `--directory=` — fix: make the target the work-tree) is a **CRITICAL, live** bug filed separately, fixed on `main` independent of retirement (it also covers copy-mode's non-git apply). **Landed 2026-07-01, commit 35c8c576** (cwd=target + throwaway `--git-dir`, `--unsafe-paths` dropped); drained to findings-resolved.md. **Op-F1 (stopped-overlay gate-deadlock) — RESOLVED as a pre-upgrade audit.** A correct merged view comes only from a live overlayfs mount; the new binary can't create it (mount code deleted), a plain start reads only the lower (no agent changes), and offline host-side reconstruction needs `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` to read overlayfs `trusted.overlay.*` opaque/redirect xattrs (so the unprivileged binary can't reconstruct deletions correctly, nor detect when it's wrong → silent incorrectness, refused). So a stopped overlay sandbox genuinely **cannot** be recovered post-upgrade. The **dry-run plan enumerates affected stopped overlay sandboxes and branches by backend**: **Linux-stopped** is recoverable (upper persists host-side) → downgrade, start it (the container survives the binary swap), re-upgrade, re-migrate; **macOS-stopped** is **already lost** (DF69 — tmpfs upper gone at stop), no recovery path. The user may instead **proceed** destructively (abandons the changes; Linux upper **dropped, not trashed** — the flatten doesn't stash secret-bearing deltas, matching the trash clause below). The gate blocking `start` post-upgrade is *why* this is a pre-upgrade audit, not an in-migration action — not a bug to engineer around. **Trash cluster — RESOLVED.** The promotion *moves* unchanged items, so `_^^_orig`→`trash/` holds only old versions of *changed* items, never the unchanged bulk (the "2× full data in trash" fear, re-audit F3, was wrong). A18 is reconciled: the general default is **trash-retain** (decision 3; small), not "deleted." The **overlay flatten drops** its `_^^_orig` (the old upper — redundant with the new copy, revivable only with the old binary, secret-bearing) rather than trashing it, which erases the secrets-at-rest and trash-conflation concerns for the flatten and matches the abandon case (the displaced upper is dropped/lost, not stashed). The `prune`-gated-during-migration disk wrinkle is a known minor residual, not worth handling at current scale (<200 users). **Crash-safety state-machine — RESOLVED (mechanical).** Folded into Promotion/Recovery: **(a)** the promotion **reorders to rename `U`→`U_^^_orig` *before* moving the build to `U_^^_new`**, so `U_^^_new` never coexists with a live `U` — this *dissolves* the unenumerated `{U + partial-new}` state (re-audit #1); its replacement, `{U_^^_orig}` alone, is trivially recovered (rename back). **(b)** the `.schema_version` marker is written **durably (temp+fsync+rename)** so presence implies complete contents (#2); **(c)** repopulate uses the **structural filter `entries(orig)\entries(new)`**, never re-running the transform (#3); **(d)** a **granularity rule** (rebuild any dir whose children changed) keeps the flatten's overlay artifacts in `_^^_orig` to be dropped, not carried forward (#4 — no separate delete-list needed); **(e)** **fsync the source dir per repopulate move** (POSIX rename isn't crash-ordered across two dir updates), overturning the earlier "moves are exempt" (#5); **(f)** a **`build-complete` sentinel** (fsync'd last in the build) defends against a partial move-in if the same-FS guard was wrong (#6); **(g)** recovery rules **split by unit kind** — realm = `.schema_version`, sandbox = on-disk `Mode` form (#7). The gate's "completes it" wording is corrected to "resolves per the state table (promote / resume / **discard-and-restore**)."

**Final re-audit batch — RESOLVED.** **P1/P2 (plan/apply destructive-set drift):** two layered guards — (i) **newly-destructive ⇒ refuse** (a sandbox destructive at apply but not approved as such is skipped + reported, stamp not flipped, never silently abandoned), and (ii) a **beta-only widen-guard inventory** — `migrate --dry-run` records the destructive set (stopped overlay sandboxes) to `library/overlay-inventory.jsonl`; a destructive run **fails iff that set *widened*** (a victim not reviewed), proceeds on a shrink, treats a missing inventory as empty (so review is effectively required), deletes it unconditionally, and retires with `:overlay`. **`--yes` de-overloaded:** it authorizes only the benign migration; abandoning a stopped overlay sandbox needs the explicit **`--abandon-stopped-overlay`**. `--check` stays pure read-only; `--dry-run` writes the throwaway inventory. **F7:** sandboxes-root is resolved from the **schema version** (records whether relocation ran), not directory existence — so an empty/stale `library/sandboxes/` can't shadow a populated top-level one; orphans from a botched prior migration (A6) are explicitly out of scope. **C2:** the flatten **stops the container immediately after its swap** (the capture already has the merged view; the running container would otherwise hold a stale overlay mount over `:copy` on-disk form) and the CLI reports which running sandboxes it stopped. **F2:** the flatten **requires the agent idle** and **fails with a useful message** if it is active, rather than capturing a torn tree. **F8 (fleet-brick) dismissed:** a cross-host shared `~/.yoloai` is already broken by per-host runtime state (container IDs, VM handles, sockets), so migration isn't what breaks it — no warning warranted.

**Fix-audit (2026-07-01) — a round auditing the fixes themselves found real holes in them (incl. three I introduced); these clauses SUPERSEDE the corresponding details above.** **(1) Repopulate keeps `_^^_orig` whole** (reflink/copy, never move): the "fsync the source dir per move" fix (clause (e) above) was **backwards** — `fsync(orig)` forces the source-unlink durable while deferring the dest-add, stranding the item in neither dir; keeping the source intact until the swap dissolves that loss (re-audit F-1) and the marker-laundering of it (F-2) by construction. **(2) Per-realm ready-marker:** a **per-sandbox** unit has no `.schema_version`; its durable ready-marker is the **`Mode=copy` flip in `_^^_new/environment.json`**, written last (F-3). Recovery is exhaustive over all 8 name-sets with a **halt-and-surface catch-all** for the invariant-excluded ones, and the discard path is **order-load-bearing** (remove `_^^_new` before restoring `U`) (F-4). **(3) Flatten capture is an in-container `cp` of the kernel-assembled merged mount only** — the "reconstruct host-side from lower+upper (Linux)" route is **struck** (it needs `CAP_SYS_ADMIN` for `trusted.overlay.*`; F1-A). **(4) DF70 fix lever is `cwd=target`**, not the **inert** `--work-tree` (git apply writes relative to CWD; a `--work-tree`-only fix silently lands nothing) (F2-A). **(5) Widen-guard inventory must NOT live under `library/`** (flat-v0 has no `library/` at dry-run; materializing it trips `isFlatV0Install`→false and breaks relocation) — put it under the CLI realm; membership (subset) check, not count; check-then-delete (keep on widen-fail); ∅ without `--abandon-stopped-overlay` (A1/A2/A3/A5). **(6) Op-F1 branches on host `GOOS`, not backend** (overlay is docker-only, so backend can't discriminate Linux vs macOS), and handles `Unavailable`/`Broken`/`Removed` (cannot-audit/quarantine, never proceed-abandon) (B1/B3). **(7) F7 is a closed two-case rule "caught in amber"** (no CLI stamp → root/sandboxes; stamp present → library/sandboxes; no third case ever), and the **CLI injects** the resolved root — the orchestrator can't import `cliutil` to read the CLI stamp itself (C1). **(8) Idle gate is bounded/best-effort:** warn up front, reasonable check, bounded re-verify, **soldier on with a warning if idle stays undeterminable** — never a permanent block (a wedged migration is worse than a small torn-capture risk; C3 + user steer). This closes the fix-audit.

**Resolved this round.** (1) bad-sandbox = **quarantine-or-abort, user's choice** (flag for headless, default abort); (3) **no downgrade** — migration is one-way (the `*_^^_orig` is transient, not a retained backup; escape from a persistent forward bug = per-sandbox quarantine + original-intact-until-commit; document "back up before upgrading"); (4) **hard-refuse + single-filesystem** — the whole realm + scratch must be on one local FS so every `mv` is atomic; (5) **one whole-tree live `flock`** held for the run (all other commands refuse; released on death). **Recovery model:** scratch dirs are **disposable, tossed on every invocation, never resumed** (the build phase needs no crash-recovery — only the live-dir renames do); a half-finished rename in a *live* dir (a `*_^^_new`/`*_^^_orig` sentinel) blocks everything-but-`migrate` as an independent guard. Still open: the user's further critiques — the design remains unsealed.

**Built + post-build audit (2026-07-01) — implemented, independently audited, remediated; the design is now sealed by the code.** Implemented on branch `crash-safe-migration` (off `release-prep`), pausing per phase: **P0** durable primitives (`internal/fileutil` `AtomicWriteFile`/`FsyncDir`/`FsyncTree`; `F_FULLFSYNC` on darwin); **P1** the generic crash-safe promotion + **exhaustive 8-name-set recovery classifier** (`internal/migrate/promote.go`) with crash-injection tests at every rename boundary; **P2** the `Migrator` plan/apply gate (`--yes` never abandons work, `--abandon-stopped-overlay` required, headless=abort, newly-destructive-refuse); **P3** the v3→v4 `OverlayFlatten` migrator (`internal/orchestrator/migrate_overlay.go`; raw in-container `cp -a` merged-tree capture, stamp-last); **P4** the breaking `:overlay` retirement (all `CAP_SYS_ADMIN`/mount code deleted; only `store.OverlayLowerDir` read-glue + the migrator kept). Four independent reviewers then audited P0–P4; remediation landed as five commits: **(B)** the recovery classifier tested `build-complete` *before* readiness, so a crash in `promoteReady`'s window (sentinel removed, swap not yet done) misread a finished ready build as partial and **discarded a completed migration** (no data loss — `_^^_orig` stayed pristine, so it rebuilt — but wasteful and wrong); reordered to test readiness first, making the discard branch a safe defensive fallback. **(A)** `:overlay` was **NOT docker-only** — docker, podman, **and apple** all set `OverlayDirs` and could create overlay sandboxes; the migrator hardcoded a Docker runtime, so a live podman/apple overlay sandbox read as *removed* and its uncommitted work would be silently abandoned under `--abandon-stopped-overlay`. The migrator now resolves each sandbox's runtime from its stored `environment.json` backend. **This corrects clause (6)'s "overlay is docker-only" premise;** its GOOS-branching for the stopped-overlay *recoverability message* stands (GOOS, not backend, is the right axis for macOS-lost vs Linux-recoverable). **(D)** `--check`/`--dry-run` on an un-relocated flat-v0 install scanned the empty namespaced sandboxes dir and under-reported; the CLI now audits `CurrentLibraryDataDir()` (TOP pre-relocation, TOP/library after) — the closed two-case F7 rule, resolved at the CLI edge (the library stays oblivious). **(C — verified, no change)** `F_FULLFSYNC` on an APFS *directory* fd succeeds (probed on-device), so `FsyncDir` needs no darwin fallback. **(E)** cosmetics — dropped the "reflink-aware" overclaim (that is D109 Phase 1, a separate unshipped workstream), deleted 4 unused overlay path helpers, routed the retired-overlay `tags.go` path to the migrate-first error, corrected the EXDEV comments (`os.Rename` returns EXDEV, it does not silently copy). `make check` + full orchestrator/CLI integration green after every commit; nothing pushed. DF68/DF69 drained to findings-resolved; the plan + audit docs archived.

## D111 — `:copy` preserves git history by default (gated on in-confinement git); `:copy-strict` is the opt-out + auto-fallback

**Context.** Default `:copy` honors `.gitignore` by enumerating the project through git (`git.ListProjectFiles`) and copying that file list — which never includes `.git`. So the work copy arrived with no history: `git log`/`blame`/`bisect` were impossible, and the only way to get history was `:copy-all`, which also re-copies gitignored secrets. The original reason to avoid a writable real `.git` in the work copy was the copy-mode RCE class (host-side git running agent-controlled `filter`/`textconv`/`fsmonitor` drivers — see [copy-mode-git-rce.md](../archive/plans/copy-mode-git-rce.md)). That reason is dissolved wherever work-copy git runs **in confinement** (`runtime.GitRunsInConfinement`: docker/podman/containerd via `GitExecInConfinement`, tart via SandboxSide).

**Decision.**
- **(a)** Default `:copy` now **CoW-clones the source `.git`** alongside the gitignore-filtered working tree, gated on `GitRunsInConfinement(rt)`. `createCopyBaseline` already records HEAD when a repo is present, so the baseline logic is unchanged. The gitignored-file strip is retained (orthogonal hygiene). On a backend that does **not** confine work-copy git (apple/seatbelt today — see [confine-host-side-git.md](../design/plans/confine-host-side-git.md)), the copy **auto-falls-back to strip** with a one-time `slog.Warn` — preserving a writable real `.git` there would widen the host-side RCE surface.
- **(b)** `:copy-strict` is the explicit opt-out (strip `.git`, fresh baseline = the old behavior) on **all three surfaces**: the `:copy-strict` dir suffix, the `--copy-strict` flag, and the profile `copy_strict:` key. Precedence: explicit dir suffix > `--copy-strict` flag > profile `copy_strict`. Persisted as `StripHistory` in `store.DirEnvironment` so reset reproduces the decision. `:copy-all` (which already copies `.git`) is unchanged and cannot be combined with strict.
- **(c) Secrets-in-history rationale.** Preserving history only *newly* exposes secrets that were **committed then removed** (present in history, absent from HEAD) — currently-tracked secrets are already in the work copy, and never-committed gitignored secrets stay stripped either way. That committed-then-removed set is the already-compromised "rotate it" case (it's in every clone/fork/backup), so stripping it from one sandbox copy is theater; `copy-strict` is the home for the rare not-yet-rotated case, not the default.

**Status.** Linux/cross-platform core implemented + unit-tested (2026-07-04); create/reset/diff/apply E2E on real Docker is pending (the dev sandbox has no daemon). macOS backends auto-strict until confine-host-side-git lands. See [copy-mode-history.md](../design/plans/copy-mode-history.md).

## D112 — Mandatory-infrastructure test policy: an absent platform-possible backend/tool FAILS, never silently skips

**Context.** The test infra quietly undercut DEV §10 / TEST §6 ("never skip a test to land code"). Two layers skipped when platform-possible infrastructure was merely *absent*: every backend's integration `TestMain` returned 0 ("… unavailable, skipping") when its daemon was down, containerd's per-test gate `t.Skipf`'d, `make integration` swallowed Docker absence, and the smoke harness had a lenient base tier that skipped missing backends (only `smoketest-full` failed loudly). A silent skip hides a misconfiguration — a stopped daemon, an absent socket, a sandbox built without nesting — exactly the environment defect the test is there to catch. This is the same failure mode as the `sandbox allowed` bug (the fix there was to make the code backend-free, not to skip).

**Decision (maintainer, 2026-07-05).**
- **If it's technically possible to run the infrastructure on this machine, running it is mandatory.** Absence/misconfiguration of a platform-possible backend FAILS loudly. The tier selects only *breadth* (which backends are in scope), never skip-vs-fail.
- **The sole carve-out is `YOLOAI_TEST_UNCONTROLLED_BACKENDS`** — a CSV of backends a runner *we don't own* (e.g. GitHub CI) genuinely cannot provision, applied per-step in the CI we control. A listed backend downgrades to skip on absence; any backend NOT listed stays mandatory even in CI (so an unexpected loss of Docker still fails). The name signals intent, not permission; empty/unset (every dev machine) ⇒ everything mandatory. We do **not** auto-detect `CI`/`GITHUB_ACTIONS`.
- **Structural impossibility ≠ absence.** A (test × backend) pairing no host change can make runnable (macOS backends on Linux; gVisor honoring iptables) is *excluded from scheduling* or compiled out by build tag — not skipped, and not subject to the carve-out. The macOS `TestMain`s keep a `runtime.GOOS` exit-0 guard on non-darwin for exactly this reason.
- **One shared helper** (`internal/testutil/uncontrolled.go`): `UncontrolledBackends` (single env read), `BackendAbsent` (TestMain exit code), `RequireBackend` (per-test). The **same env var** governs the Python smoke harness (`uncontrolled_backends()`), so the two layers stay in lockstep.
- **Smoke tiers renamed:** `smoketest` = the whole OS matrix (strict, root-escalating on Linux; was `smoketest-full`); `smoketest-quick` = the docker/container core path only (strict, no root); `smoketest-full` removed.
- **Dev tooling (`rsync`/`python3`/`uv`) is required, no carve-out** — it installs everywhere including CI, and the Python surface is app code, so `make check` now requires `uv`; the rsync/python3 test gates FAIL (not skip) on absence.

Supersedes the base-tier smoke-skip and the per-backend "unavailable → skip" gates. Ties to DEV §10 and TEST §6 (which this makes the infra *enforce*). See `docs/contributors/design/plans/mandatory-infra-test-policy.md`.

## D113 — confine work-copy git on apple + seatbelt (close the last host-side copy-mode RCE)

**Context.** The 2026-06-29 fix ([copy-mode-git-rce.md](../archive/plans/copy-mode-git-rce.md)) moved copy-mode work-copy git into confinement for docker/podman/containerd/tart, but left **apple** and **seatbelt** running work-copy git **on the host with filter/textconv/fsmonitor drivers live** — a host RCE via an agent-planted `.git/config` driver. apple's gap was undocumented (it already exposed `container exec` but never implemented `GitExecer`). The invariant this establishes: **no git operation touches an agent-writable `.git`/work-tree outside the agent's confinement.** See [confine-host-side-git.md](../design/plans/confine-host-side-git.md) (design) and [confine-host-side-git-macos-build.md](../archive/plans/confine-host-side-git-macos-build.md) (build brief).

**Decision.**
- **(a) apple** — implement `runtime.GitExecer` on the apple `Runtime`, dispatching work-copy git into its per-container VM via the existing `container exec` (untrimmed stdout, `*ExecError` on non-zero, `ErrNotRunning` when down), and set `GitExecInConfinement: true`. Mirrors the container backends; the shared base image already ships git. This closes apple with the mechanism already proven for docker.
- **(b) seatbelt** — seatbelt has no container to exec into, so "in confinement" means running **git itself under `sandbox-exec`**: `GitExec` runs `sandbox-exec -f <gitProfile> <realGit> <hardening> -C <workDir> <args>`, so any filter git spawns inherits the profile. Set `GitExecInConfinement: true`. It does **not** require the sandbox keepalive to be running (the work copy is a plain host dir + the profile is self-contained), preserving diff-on-stopped-sandbox behavior.
- **(c) A dedicated tight git profile, NOT the agent profile** (`seatbelt.GenerateGitProfile`). The agent profile grants `(allow mach-lookup)` and `(allow process-exec)` unrestricted — sandbox-escape vectors. git needs far less, so the git op gets its own profile: `(deny default)`; **`mach-lookup` left denied** (empirically git/filters/git-lfs need none); **`file-write*` scoped to the work copy only** (a broad temp write grant re-opens containment — a malicious filter would write a marker to `/tmp`); **`process-exec` confined to tool dirs** (system bins, `/opt/homebrew`, the toolchain Developer dir), never the work copy. This bounds a malicious filter to container-equivalent blast radius.
- **(d) Bypass the `/usr/bin/git` xcrun shim.** It re-invokes `xcrun`/`xcodebuild`, which need per-user temp + `mach-lookup` and cannot run under the tight profile; `resolveGitBinary` resolves the real toolchain git via `xcrun -f git` and execs that directly. Recorded in `backend-idiosyncrasies.md`.

**Verification (real macOS 26, Apple Silicon).** Malicious-filter integration tests extended to apple + seatbelt (`internal/orchestrator/integration_macos_test.go`, teeth-checked: confinement off ⇒ host marker leaks, on ⇒ contained); seatbelt profile containment battery (`runtime/seatbelt/gitprofile_test.go`): legit clean-filter + git-lfs round-trip byte-correct, malicious filter cannot write off-tree or exec a dropped payload.

**Interaction with D111.** D111's `:copy` history preservation is gated on `GitRunsInConfinement(rt)` and auto-falls-back to strip on non-confining backends "(apple/seatbelt today)". With this decision those two now confine git, so the fallback no longer triggers for them — `:copy` history preservation extends to apple/seatbelt automatically. The **broken-metadata probe** residual (`status.ProbeWorkData` host `git status`, clean-filter vector) is unchanged and remains tracked in `findings-unresolved.md`; exploitability is low (`.meta` lives outside the sandbox).

## D114 — Host artifacts get an identity-keyed orphan sweep; teardown kills before it deletes the key

**Date:** 2026-07-06. **Status:** Active — plan approved, implementing on branch `host-artifact-reclamation`. Plan: [host-artifact-reclamation.md](../archive/plans/host-artifact-reclamation.md). Addresses DF73–DF76; surfaced by a post-v0.7.0-testing disk-leak investigation.

**Context.** With `yoloai ls` reporting **no sandboxes**, the dev host still held a 4-day-old root `yoloai __inject` broker process (listening socket, `exe` already `(deleted)`), two leaked CNI netns (`yoloai-yoloai-{x,kreach}`, each with a `tap0_kata`), and 5.5 GB of retired-microvm library debris. None was reclaimable by `system prune`; `doctor` reported healthy. **Root cause:** host-side artifacts are torn down only on the happy path, **keyed off per-sandbox state files** (`injector.json`, `cni-state.json`, the sandbox dir) that can vanish independently of the artifact — a crash between artifact-create and state-write, a SIGKILL/timeout, or the `create`-replace path deleting the dir *before* killing the process. `prune` enumerates only **containers/VMs by `com.yoloai.*` labels** (+ images/volumes/caches/locks/temp); `doctor` runs backend health + a dry-run of that same container-scoped prune. Neither enumerates host processes, netns, bridges, or tmux — so once the state file is gone the artifact is invisible and permanent.

**Decision.**
- **(a) An identity-keyed host-orphan sweep** — the load-bearing principle: *a host artifact whose identity is encoded in a name/path must be reconcilable by enumerating it directly and diffing against the sandbox registry (`SandboxesDir()`), not by trusting a per-sandbox state file to still exist.* Wired into `system prune` (so `doctor`'s dry-run surfaces it for free). Three classes: **broker processes** (walk `/proc` for `<binary> __inject`, keep those whose PID is in a live sandbox's `injector.json`, reap the rest — Linux+macOS); **netns + IPAM leases** (`/var/run/netns/yoloai-yoloai-*` → sandbox name via `netnsNameFor`, delete unowned + its CNI lease — containerd/Linux; the shared `yoloai0` bridge stays, it is intentionally persistent); **seatbelt host tmux** (`<sandboxDir>/tmux/tmux.sock` with no owner → `kill-server` — darwin). Follows the proven `killStaleKataShims`/tart-census pattern, generalized. **Scoping caveat:** the broker carries no data-dir in argv, so the sweep is correct for the single-data-dir default but could over-reap across data dirs — documented, deferred to the D62 multi-principal daemon (sibling of DF45).
- **(b) Kill-before-delete ordering** (stop *creating* unreapable orphans): the `create`-replace path calls `stopInjector` + CNI teardown **before** `launch.Teardown` deletes the sandbox dir (mirroring `lifecycle.destroy`); containerd teardown runs `deleteNetNS` **before** `forceRemoveAll(sandboxDir)`. Phase 1 is the backstop; this stops the leak at the source.
- **(c) No speculative library-debris sweep.** `library/microvm/` was **never in a released schema** (unmerged spike, D104) — it cannot exist on a real install, so there is nothing for released users to clean. A general "remove unknown `library/` subdirs" pass is **rejected** (YAGNI; risks deleting future legit dirs). A *released* retired backend already has its idiom — a **library-schema migrator** (how `:overlay` retirement removed its state, `migrate_overlay.go`). The dev-host microvm tree is a one-off manual `rm`.

**Rejected.** (1) *Persisting all host-side handles into `environment.json` and reaping from that* — the record would still be deleted with the sandbox dir on the very crash paths that orphan the artifact; direct enumeration by identity-encoding name is robust to a lost record, the record is not. (2) *A general unknown-`library/`-subdir GC* — see (c). (3) *Binding the broker's lifetime to the container (die-with-parent)* — deliberately rejected at broker design time (the agent must outlive the CLI); the sweep is the right layer.

**Consequences.** `system prune` gains a host-artifact reconciler and `doctor` reports leaked host state instead of a false all-clear. Teardown ordering closes the create-replace and netns-delete-fail holes. The disk-reclaim burden that was manual (`reference_disk_reclaim_recipes` recipes 2/4) shrinks toward `prune`. The multi-data-dir broker-scoping limitation is the one carried caveat. **Composition:** builds on D62 (label orphan identity — this extends orphan-hood to non-container host artifacts), D105/D106 (the broker whose lifecycle this reaps), and the DF45 shared-state-scoping question.

**Follow-up (out of scope).** Signal-handler teardown on SIGTERM (best-effort injector/netns kill on Ctrl-C) — a separate subsystem; SIGKILL stays Phase-1's job.

## D115 — Credential broker generalized to per-agent redirect; wired for Gemini + Codex

**Date:** 2026-07-14. **Status:** Active — implementing on branch `broker-generalize-agents`. Refines [D105](#d105--egress-proxy-workstream-d-brokering-is-the-default-containment-is-opt-in-phased-by-credential-material-refines-d90d95)/[D106](#d106--egress-proxy-the-key-injectors-lifetime-is-a-pluggable-host-cli-sidecar--embedder-in-process-recovery-is-lazy-re-derivation-refines-d105). Addresses DF82. Grounded in source-verified research of each CLI's base-URL/auth mechanism (Gemini `@google/genai`, Codex Rust `main`, plus Aider/OpenCode for the deferred bucket).

**Context.** The broker (D105/D106) was built deliberately general — a struct-driven `{UpstreamURL, Destination, Credentials[{EnvVar,Header,Prefix}], BaseURLEnvVar, AuthTokenEnvVar}` model — but only the `claude` agent ever populated `Broker`. So `gemini`/`codex`/`opencode`/`aider` delivered their API keys **directly into the sandbox** (the pre-D105 baseline), contradicting the "isolate the environment, not just files" thesis (DF82). Two things blocked pure-declarative reuse: (1) `buildInjectorSpec` **hardcoded** `StripHeaders: []string{"Authorization"}` — the header the agent carries its placeholder in — which is Claude-specific (Gemini uses `x-goog-api-key`); (2) the redirect was env-var-only (`BaseURLEnvVar`), but Codex's CLI has **no base-URL env var** — current Codex (Rust `main`) reads `openai_base_url` only from `~/.codex/config.toml`.

**Decision.**
- **(a) Per-agent placeholder header.** New `BrokerConfig.PlaceholderHeader`; `buildInjectorSpec` now emits `StripHeaders: []string{bc.PlaceholderHeader}`. Claude/Codex set `"Authorization"`, Gemini `"x-goog-api-key"`. No behavior change for Claude.
- **(b) Two redirect delivery channels, exactly one per agent.** Keep `BaseURLEnvVar` (env redirect: Claude `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL`, Gemini `GOOGLE_GEMINI_BASE_URL`). Add `ConfigFiles []BrokerConfigFile{RelPath, Patch(current,baseURL,placeholder)}` for CLIs that read their base URL **and/or credential** only from config files. The launch path (`patchBrokerConfigFiles`) patches each file host-side in the sandbox's `agent-runtime/` dir **after** the injector endpoint is resolved (knowable only at launch) and **before** the container starts — the dir is bind-mounted, so the agent reads the patched files at launch. The reconcile path rebinds the recorded port, so the persisted values stay valid without a rewrite.
- **(c) Gemini** (env, single-provider): upstream `generativelanguage.googleapis.com`, header `x-goog-api-key`. It has no dedicated token var, so the placeholder rides in `GEMINI_API_KEY` itself (`AuthTokenEnvVar == GEMINI_API_KEY`; `applyBrokerEnv` drops-then-sets). Verified live.
- **(d) Codex** (file, single-provider): two files, because Codex 0.144 reads **neither** its base URL nor its key from an env var (empirically verified on the shipped CLI — the research described older behavior). **config.toml**: set the single top-level `openai_base_url` on the built-in openai provider (chosen over a custom `[model_providers.*]` block — one key, preserves the user's model/approval settings). The patch appends `/v1`, so Codex's `{base_url}/responses` (Responses API — chat/completions removed Feb 2026) resolves to the real `/v1/responses`. **auth.json**: write the placeholder as `{auth_mode:"apikey", OPENAI_API_KEY:<placeholder>}` — a bare `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`CODEX_API_KEY` env var leaves Codex "not logged in" (this was the first, incorrect attempt); it reads a logged-in `auth.json`. Codex hits `{base_url}/responses` over a **WebSocket** (baked in, HTTPS fallback) — the injector (a Go `httputil.ReverseProxy`) proxies the WS upgrade AND injects the real key on the handshake, verified by a dedicated test and a live run (placeholder auth.json → "Logged in using an API key" → WS to the injector). TOML round-trip via a new direct dep, `pelletier/go-toml/v2` (compiled into the binary — not a runtime dep, so the "binary + Docker only" rule holds).
- **(e) API-key path only.** For every agent, brokering engages only when a brokerable **API key** is present (`SelectCredential`). A subscription/OAuth login (Gemini "Login with Google", Codex ChatGPT) ignores the base-URL override and is left to direct delivery — the same limitation class as Claude's existing broker, stated honestly, not silently broken. (Aside surfaced here: with an API key but no host `~/.codex/auth.json`, **direct-delivery** Codex was already broken pre-D115 — the env var doesn't authenticate it; the brokered path now generates a working `auth.json`. The direct-delivery gap is noted, not fixed in this pass.)

**Rejected / deferred.**
- **Multi-provider agents (aider, opencode) deferred.** Both are per-provider (no single redirect knob): each provider needs its own upstream, base-URL target, and header convention (`x-api-key`/`Bearer`/`x-goog-api-key`), so brokering them needs a **multi-upstream** generalization (N injector bindings + per-provider routing) the single-fixed-upstream injector doesn't do yet. OpenCode additionally carries an unresolved upstream bug (custom `baseURL` drops the Anthropic key, sst/opencode #21737). Reparked under DF82 with verified per-provider specs.
- **Custom `model_providers` block for Codex** — more config written and it overrides the user's active provider; the single `openai_base_url` key is the minimal correct redirect.
- **AWS / non-LLM resources = separate workstream.** Already reserved in the closed `credential` sets (`RequestSigner{aws-sigv4}`, `MintingSource`, both `ErrNotImplemented`) per D105's survey; needs request-signing + the injector fronting non-LLM hosts. Out of DF82's scope.

**Consequences.** Brokering is now the **default for Gemini and Codex** API-key users (key stays host-side; transparent for a valid API key). Direct delivery still applies on OAuth-only auth, `--no-broker`, `--network-none`, or a backend that can't host an injector. A new invariant test (`TestBrokerConfig_WellFormed`) guards every brokerable agent's config so a future edit can't ship a broken redirect. **Composition:** builds directly on D105/D106; the multi-provider and request-signing extensions remain the reserved next steps.

## D116 — `AGENTS.md` is the agent contract; `CLAUDE.md`'s condensed principle restatement retires into the formal system

**Date:** 2026-07-15. **Status:** Active — implementing on branch `contributor-docs-sweep`. Establishes `general-principles.md` §15. Prompted by PR #36, the project's first outside code contribution to merge.

**Context.** yoloAI was a solo project until 2026-07-14. Its conventions lived in the maintainer's head and in `CLAUDE.md` — which **only Claude Code reads**. PR #36 was prepared with Codex, which reads `AGENTS.md`; the repo had none. It then missed two rules recorded nowhere a contributor could see: a user-visible break needs a `docs/BREAKING-CHANGES.md` entry in the same PR (PR #36 made `yoloai config set backend docker` exit 1 across nine changed files, none of them that one — the entry existed only because review asked), and invalidating a name obliges sweeping the surfaces that mirror it (the help topic advertised `backend` for all 15 releases from v0.2.0 to v0.8.0 after the March 2026 rename, `make check` green throughout). Neither miss was the contributor's fault. The maintainer's own follow-up then misfiled the breaking-changes entry into a shipped section — twice — because the `## Unreleased` convention existed only in the shape of three stamp commits (`883600b9`, `00f5a046`, `3c72c962`).

A 44-agent convention audit (adversarially verified, streams over git history / docs / hooks / CI) confirmed 25 conventions existing only in practice. Its own completeness critic found the audit had missed the one rule PR #36 actually broke — evidence for §15's both-directions rule, and the reason this entry exists rather than a larger one.

**Decision.**
- **(a) `AGENTS.md` at the repo root is the canonical instruction file for every coding agent.** `CLAUDE.md` imports it via `@AGENTS.md`. Verified against the Claude Code docs: Claude Code does **not** read `AGENTS.md` natively and there is no flag that changes this; the import is the officially documented bridge, and is preferred over a `CLAUDE.md → AGENTS.md` symlink, which needs Admin/Developer Mode on Windows.
